A study by OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a migrant workers' organisation) found that 94% of women workers forced to return had suffered regular physical and mental abuse abroad and 47% sexual harassment, according to a Prothom Alo editorial of 19 October 2026 <sup>[N143](#n143)</sup>.

**How the way home works in Saudi Arabia:** officials said in January 2024 that most women who leave a household before the two-year contract ends run away to the embassy's safe home, are moved from there to the Saudi government's safe home, and are flown home at the Saudi government's cost; a woman who wants to go back within three months of arriving is sent home through the Saudi company that recruited her <sup>[N28 (2024)](#n28)</sup>.

**A case:** Lutfa Begum of Brahmanbaria went to Saudi Arabia as a domestic worker in March 2020 and worked 1 month 5 days, in two neighbouring houses where she said the men behaved badly; the person who sent her did not find her other work, and when she asked to go home she was sent to a shelter, where she stayed about three months <sup>[N28 (2024)](#n28)</sup>. She said she was paid nothing, and after coming home received Tk 30,000 in cash help in two instalments <sup>[N28 (2024)](#n28)</sup>.

**Counselling:** the Cumilla manpower office's assistant director said in April 2026 that there is almost no psychological counselling for migrant workers, especially women <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>.

<a id="can-women-get-garment-jobs-in-jordan"></a>

#### 1.8.10 Can women get garment jobs in Jordan?

Yes, through BOESL: its November 2026 notice took women aged 20 to 35 who can run plain and overlock sewing machines, at no cost to the worker <sup>[25](#o25)</sup>. In June 2026 the CID arrested three people who used a Jordan garment company's name and seal to take Tk 2 to 2.5 lakh a person without a licence <sup>[N144](#n144)</sup>. Steps: see the Jordan doc, Work visa: garment and factory worker.

<a id="problems-and-coming-home"></a>

### 1.9 Problems and coming home

<a id="what-does-the-migrant-insurance-cover"></a>

#### 1.9.1 What does the migrant insurance cover?

Workers who left with BMET clearance from 1 January 2023 are insured for Tk 10 lakh by Jiban Bima Corporation (JBC), for workers aged 18 to 55, for a one-time Tk 1,000 <sup>[110 (2024)](#o110)</sup>. The cover lasts 5 years from the smart card's date <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. It pays the full Tk 10 lakh on death or total permanent disability, 10% to 90% for partial disability, and Tk 50,000 if you lose the job and return within 6 months of going <sup>[34](#o34)</sup>. Job loss is not covered if you went entirely at the employer's cost <sup>[110 (2024)](#o110)</sup>. WEWB helps the worker or family claim <sup>[111](#o111)</sup>.

**Death after the cover ends, and how the money is paid.** Under the insurance contract of 12 March 2023, a worker who dies naturally or in an accident during the cover period, or within 90 days after it ends, is paid the full Tk 10 lakh, and when a worker dies abroad the money goes to the worker's bank account after the body comes home, without any application, according to the Welfare Board and JBC in January 2024 <sup>[N137 (2024)](#n137)</sup>.

**What it does not pay for.** Under the same contract, as Prothom Alo reported in January 2024, there is no insurance benefit for <sup>[N137 (2024)](#n137)</sup>:
- suicide or self-harm within six months of cover starting;
- death or illness from HIV/AIDS or a similar disease;
- death in dangerous sports or adventure activities;
- death from alcohol or drug addiction;
- death in war, a riot or an attack by criminals;
- execution under a court sentence.

**In practice.** By JBC's count, 32,24,343 workers went abroad from the start of the compulsory migrant insurance in late 2019 to the end of 2023, and only 1,397 workers or families claimed on it; by the Welfare Board's 2022-23 annual report, Tk 31 crore 72 lakh 10 thousand in death insurance was paid in FY2022-23 to the families of 860 workers <sup>[N137 (2024)](#n137)</sup>. Disability and early-return benefits must be claimed, the government agencies hold no information on workers who come back disabled or within six months, and people involved said there is no special effort to tell workers about the insurance before they leave, Prothom Alo reported in January 2024 <sup>[N137 (2024)](#n137)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where and with which papers does a worker who came home disabled or within six months claim the insurance, and how long does JBC take to pay <sup>[N137 (2024)](#n137)</sup>?

<a id="if-a-worker-dies-abroad-what-does-the-family-get"></a>

#### 1.9.2 If a worker dies abroad, what does the family get?

1. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-abroad-what-does-the-family-get-item-1"></a>**At the airport:** a Tk 35,000 cheque for transport and burial for every body that arrives, free, collected within 3 months of the body's arrival <sup>[112](#o112)</sup>.
2. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-abroad-what-does-the-family-get-item-2"></a>**From the Welfare Board:** a Tk 3 lakh grant, if the worker had BMET clearance or membership and died in the country the clearance was for <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>. If the family took the airport cheque, no separate application is needed; if the body was buried abroad, apply within 1 year <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>.
3. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-abroad-what-does-the-family-get-item-3"></a>**From the insurance:** Tk 10 lakh <sup>[110 (2024)](#o110)</sup>.
4. <a id="if-a-worker-dies-abroad-what-does-the-family-get-item-4"></a>**From the employer:** labour wings recover death compensation, wages and benefits, and the Board pays the heirs; 1,852 cases brought in Tk 127.6 crore in 2023-24 <sup>[70 (2024)](#o70)</sup>.

The grant goes to the spouse, children and parents <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>. The Cumilla Probashi Kallyan Centre's assistant director said in April 2026 that financial help for bringing a body home is given for BMET card holders, that a free ambulance takes the body from the airport, and that Tk 25,000 is paid after the body arrives and up to Tk 3 lakh in eligible cases <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>. The president of a Cumilla advocacy forum called it a major humanitarian problem that the family is not told when someone dies or goes missing abroad <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>. By Welfare Board data, in the first six months of 2026 it paid Tk 9.45 crore for burial, Tk 61.56 crore in grants and more than Tk 44 crore in insurance, and recovered about Tk 75 crore in compensation, unpaid wages and insurance from abroad; people in the sector said families must visit the Board day after day after applying <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>. The full steps are in the Qatar doc, If a worker dies, is injured or loses the job.

> **Need to know**
> - Is the airport payment for transport and burial Tk 35,000 or Tk 25,000 in 2026 <sup>[112](#o112), [N64](#n64)</sup>?

<a id="how-is-the-body-brought-home"></a>

#### 1.9.3 How is the body brought home?

The family applies to the Welfare Board, which takes their wish (body home or burial abroad) to the embassy and follows the case, free <sup>[113](#o113), [114](#o114)</sup>. The Ministry also brings home bodies of irregular, undocumented and destitute workers: the next of kin applies with the cause of death, proof of status and the death certificate; free, 7 working days <sup>[49](#o49)</sup>. A free ambulance from Dhaka and Chattogram airports is available on request <sup>[115](#o115)</sup>. The Minister said in August 2026 that the government brings home the body of any Bangladeshi who dies abroad, whether the person went legally or not, and gives the family financial help and help with the children's education <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>. Bodies can take months: eight Bangladeshis who died on a boat off Tunisia in February 2024 reached Dhaka on 2 May 2024, and went to the Dhaka Medical College morgue for post-mortem before being handed to their families <sup>[N145 (2024)](#n145)</sup>. Welfare Board officials said in October 2026 that bodies from Middle Eastern countries need approval from several offices before they can be sent, which takes some time though less than before, and that outside pandemic years under 1% of migrants who die are buried in the destination country <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>.

**The numbers.** From January to 30 September 2026, 4,060 bodies of migrant workers reached Bangladesh, more than 14 a day, Prothom Alo reported, while Welfare Board data show 3,651 bodies came home in 2019 and 3,140 in 2020 <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>. In a Welfare Board analysis of 2018 to 2022, 18,166 bodies came home, of which 3,698 died in road accidents, workplace accidents, fires, falls from buildings or electric shock, 2,649 of them in road accidents <sup>[N102](#n102)</sup>.

<a id="why-do-so-many-families-get-no-compensation-from-the-employer"></a>

#### 1.9.4 Why do so many families get no compensation from the employer?

Many deaths are recorded as "natural causes": 71% of deaths of Bangladeshis in Qatar from November 2016 to October 2020, by Amnesty International's reading of Bangladeshi government records <sup>[N146 (2021)](#n146)</sup>. Amnesty found in 2021 that without investigation or post-mortem, this rules out compensation from employers <sup>[N146 (2021)](#n146)</sup>. The Welfare Board's grant is paid anyway <sup>[68](#o68)</sup>.

<a id="what-help-is-there-if-im-injured-or-fall-ill-abroad"></a>

#### 1.9.5 What help is there if I'm injured or fall ill abroad?

The Welfare Board asks the embassy to bring a sick or injured worker home on the worker's or family's application <sup>[116](#o116)</sup>. Back home, workers with clearance or membership can get up to Tk 2 lakh for treatment, applying within 1 year of return <sup>[117](#o117)</sup>. The insurance pays for permanent disability (see [What does the migrant insurance cover?](#what-does-the-migrant-insurance-cover)) <sup>[110 (2024)](#o110)</sup>.

<a id="what-happens-if-im-arrested-abroad"></a>

#### 1.9.6 What happens if I'm arrested abroad?

The cases below are from Saudi Arabia, where Bangladeshis are the largest group of foreign workers, at least 25 lakh <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.

- **Executions:** according to ESOHR (the European Saudi Organisation for Human Rights, based in Berlin), 59 of the 108 people Saudi Arabia executed from January to June 2026 were foreigners, including 49 of the 71 executed for drug offences, and Bangladeshis were among the 108, though how many is not known <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>. Two Bangladeshis convicted of an "immoral act" in a mosque in Mecca were executed shortly before October 2026 <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>. According to Amnesty International, 597 people were executed in Saudi Arabia for drug offences alone from January 2014 to June 2025, about 75% of them foreigners, in a country whose population of about 3 crore 53 lakh is about 44% foreign <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.
- **Your rights on paper:** Saudi Arabia's 2013 Law of Criminal Procedure gives an accused person the right to legal help during both investigation and trial, and bans torture <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.
- **In practice:** ESOHR documented that death-row prisoners in Khamis Mushait, Najran and Tabuk prisons had no professional interpreter or legal help, did not understand the proceedings, and often did not know when their sentence was upheld or their plea for pardon rejected <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>. Amnesty, documenting 25 foreigners convicted of drug offences (from Egypt, Ethiopia, Jordan, Pakistan and Somalia), found many went through the system alone, without understanding or receiving the court papers or charges, without consular help, and at risk of confessions taken without an adequate interpreter or lawyer <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>. It found that low education, poor language skills and lack of money harm these migrants twice: brokers cheat or exploit them from the start of going to Saudi Arabia, and if they face legal trouble they cannot hire a good lawyer or defend themselves properly, and in most cases have less access to consular help <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>. In December 2024 the UN Committee on the Elimination of Racial Discrimination found racial and ethnic discrimination in Saudi Arabia's criminal justice system, with migrant and domestic workers, including women, subjected to arbitrary detention, torture and ill-treatment, and called on Saudi Arabia to impose a moratorium on the death penalty <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.
- **What the family is told:** Amnesty says Saudi law has no clear rule on warning the prisoner or family before an execution; families said the prisoner may at most phone 24 hours before, some prisoners were told by prison officers the day before "your turn has come", and many families learned of an execution from other prisoners' calls or from the state news agency <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.
- **The body:** Saudi law does not guarantee the body is returned after an execution; in all three of Amnesty's 25 cases executed between January and June 2025, Saudi Arabia neither returned the body nor told the family where it was buried <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.

ESOHR could learn or record nothing before the execution about 91 of the 108 people executed in early 2026, so there is no answer on how many Bangladeshis are held, on what charges, or whether they had lawyers, interpreters or consular help <sup>[N116](#n116)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - When a Bangladeshi is arrested in Saudi Arabia, does the embassy's labour wing learn of it, and does it arrange a lawyer or interpreter? Whom should the family contact first?
> - How many Bangladeshis are on death row in Saudi Arabia, and for which charges?

<a id="what-help-is-there-when-i-come-back"></a>

#### 1.9.7 What help is there when I come back?

Airport welfare desks give food and water to returnees from jail, help with customs and lost luggage, and take complaints <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>. Women back within a year after abuse or fraud get Tk 5,000 in cash <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. PKB's rehabilitation loan lends returnees up to Tk 50 lakh for a project at 9% simple interest, up to Tk 3 lakh without collateral <sup>[118](#o118)</sup>. It needs a trade licence and a guarantor, and in 2021 only about 2% of pandemic returnees got PKB's special reintegration loan <sup>[118](#o118), [N147 (2021)](#n147)</sup>. PKB's managing director, Md. Mujibur Rahman, said in March 2024 that loan conditions for returnee women had been eased: a woman whose husband or family won't take her back can borrow without a fixed address, her loan carries no service charge, and loans up to Tk 1 lakh need no equity, though few women apply <sup>[N148 (2024)](#n148)</sup>. Prothom Alo reported in March 2025 that few workers benefit from the projects begun for returnees after the pandemic <sup>[N149](#n149)</sup>. Hasan Imam of the Center for Development Communication said in December 2025 that the government's reintegration policy, begun in 2022, received final approval in 2025, with six objectives: employment and financial self-reliance, entrepreneurship, social reintegration and health care, psychosocial support, support for exploited and abused women, and sustainability <sup>[N120](#n120)</sup>. Other lenders, the RAISE reintegration project and what returnee leaders say: see Help after you get home <sup>[N107](#n107), [N82](#n82)</sup>.

**Returnees with HIV.** According to the national AIDS/STD control programme of the Directorate General of Health Services, 1,891 people were found infected with HIV in Bangladesh from December 2024 to November 2025, 12% of them people who had come back from abroad <sup>[N103](#n103)</sup>. The government supplies antiretroviral (ARV) drugs free, but counselling, mental health support and livelihood rehabilitation for returnees are very limited, Prothom Alo reported in February 2026; Mir of the youth organisation Brave Dimension Global said one deported worker got counselling not from any government agency but from a private organisation <sup>[N103](#n103)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where does a returnee with HIV get the free ARV drugs and counselling: which hospital or centre, and with which papers <sup>[N103](#n103)</sup>?

<a id="what-can-i-bring-home-without-paying-duty"></a>

#### 1.9.8 What can I bring home without paying duty?

Under the Baggage Rules 2025 a returning worker aged 12 or over may bring up to 65 kg free, plus 35 kg more of clothes and personal items <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. One of each of a list of goods is duty-free, including a laptop, an oven, a rice cooker, a fan and a TV up to 29 inches <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. You may bring 2 used mobile phones and 1 new one, the new one once a year <sup>[120](#o120)</sup>. Bigger items such as a fridge or air conditioner pay a fixed duty <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. A Bangladeshi passport holder may bring no alcohol <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. The full list is in the Qatar doc, Coming home, What you can bring home.

<a id="how-much-gold-and-cash-can-i-bring"></a>

#### 1.9.9 How much gold and cash can I bring?

Up to 100 g of gold or 200 g of silver jewellery in all, declared, once a year, with no more than 12 pieces of one type, is duty-free <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. One gold bar of up to 117 g (10 tola) is allowed once a year if declared, at Tk 5,000 duty per 11.664 g; anything more, or a hidden bar, is confiscated <sup>[119](#o119), [120](#o120)</sup>. Up to USD 10,000 in foreign currency needs no declaration <sup>[121 (2020)](#o121)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Do customs officers still apply USD 5,000, the figure on Bangladesh Customs' own page <sup>[122](#o122)</sup>?

<a id="is-there-help-for-my-children"></a>

#### 1.9.10 Is there help for my children?

The Welfare Board pays scholarships to migrants' high-achieving children (Tk 27,500 a year at SSC level, Tk 34,000 at HSC level) and reserves 0.5% of HSC admission seats for them <sup>[70 (2024)](#o70)</sup>.

<a id="myths"></a>

### 1.10 Myths

<a id="a-free-visa-lets-me-work-for-anyone"></a>

#### 1.10.1 "A free visa lets me work for anyone."

Brokers sell it as "go and work wherever you like," for Tk 5 to 7 lakh <sup>[N150](#n150)</sup>. In fact a Saudi employer may not employ a worker in a profession other than the one on the work permit <sup>[78](#o78)</sup>, and buying and working on a free visa is illegal and punishable in Qatar <sup>[66 (2021)](#o66)</sup>. The company named can cancel the visa at any time, making the worker illegal <sup>[N151](#n151)</sup>. Of 10 men one Cumilla broker sent to Saudi Arabia, three got a proper job with a contract (2024) <sup>[N152 (2024)](#n152)</sup>.

<a id="a-visit-visa-can-be-changed-to-a-work-visa-there"></a>

#### 1.10.2 "A visit visa can be changed to a work visa there."

- **Saudi Arabia:** a visit visa can't be converted to an iqama, and offers to convert one for a fee are fraud <sup>[N153 (2022)](#n153)</sup>.
- **Qatar:** you must leave and re-enter on a work visa <sup>[123](#o123)</sup>.
- **Oman:** conversion inside the country stopped for all nationalities on 31 October 2023 <sup>[N10 (2023)](#n10)</sup>.
- **The UAE:** a visit visa gives no right to work, and working on one brings fines <sup>[124](#o124)</sup>.

Working on a tourist or Umrah visa in Saudi Arabia brings a ban of 3 to 10 years <sup>[N129](#n129)</sup>.

<a id="the-agency-guarantees-the-job"></a>

#### 1.10.3 "The agency guarantees the job."

The Bangladesh High Commission in the Maldives warned in October 2026 against paying anyone who promises a guaranteed job or a quick work permit <sup>[N2](#n2)</sup>. A Bangladeshi construction worker in the UAE told a 2019 study that the contract signed in Bangladesh differs from the one the company keeps <sup>[N154 (2019)](#n154)</sup>. In Cambodia, 98% of returnees BRAC studied found no job <sup>[N26](#n26)</sup>.

<a id="pay-a-little-extra-and-the-medical-will-be-fit"></a>

#### 1.10.4 "Pay a little extra and the medical will be fit."

A bought "fit" result can still get you deported: a worker who paid Tk 25,000 for one was found unfit in Saudi Arabia and sent home <sup>[N95](#n95)</sup>.

<a id="pay-extra-and-youll-pass-the-skill-test"></a>

#### 1.10.5 "Pay extra and you'll pass the skill test."

There is no way to pass Saudi Arabia's SVP for extra money; a worker caught paying has the account locked <sup>[64](#o64), [65](#o65)</sup>.

<a id="saudi-arabia-abolished-the-kafala"></a>

#### 1.10.6 "Saudi Arabia abolished the kafala."

The kafala (sponsorship system) still stands: the October 2025 story began in an article by a UK think tank and cited no Saudi source <sup>[N155](#n155)</sup>.

<a id="the-iqama-is-the-workers-cost"></a>

#### 1.10.7 "The iqama is the worker's cost."

The employer pays the iqama, the work permit and their renewals <sup>[125](#o125)</sup>. A Bangladesh embassy official confirmed the employer should pay, though employers push it onto workers <sup>[N156](#n156)</sup>.

<a id="if-i-have-a-job-my-visa-is-safe"></a>

#### 1.10.8 "If I have a job, my visa is safe."

In the UAE an employer can cancel a visa online without the worker knowing <sup>[N157](#n157)</sup>. The UAE began cancelling many Bangladeshis' visas in July 2026, and on 13 October 2026 the Law Minister said 5,000 had been cancelled <sup>[N158](#n158), [N159](#n159)</sup>.

<a id="malaysia-has-opened-again"></a>

#### 1.10.9 "Malaysia has opened again."

In early September 2026 the Ministry warned that the process of sending workers to Malaysia was not final, that it would be announced by notice, and that no one should pay anyone before then <sup>[N160](#n160)</sup>. Recruitment was announced on 15 October 2026, but on 4 November 2026 the minister said the medical tests it needs had not yet been approved <sup>[7](#o7), [N9](#n9)</sup>. BOESL ran its own call for 112 DHL jobs (see [Can I go through the government agency](#can-i-go-through-the-government-agency-boesl), BOESL?) <sup>[41](#o41)</sup>.

<a id="oman-is-opening-soon"></a>

#### 1.10.10 "Oman is opening soon."

In August 2026 the Bangladesh Embassy in Muscat said Oman had made no formal announcement on recruiting Bangladeshis, and in September 2026 the State Minister said visas remain restricted <sup>[N161](#n161), [N12](#n12)</sup>.

<a id="kyrgyzstan-is-a-door-to-europe"></a>

#### 1.10.11 "Kyrgyzstan is a door to Europe."

A man who paid a broker Tk 4.8 lakh on a promise of Italy was taken to Bishkek and spent 44 days in a Central Asian prison <sup>[N162](#n162)</sup>.

<a id="dalal-scams-the-usual-tricks-and-how-to-spot-them"></a>

## 2 Dalal scams: the usual tricks and how to spot them

<a id="in-one-minute"></a>

### 2.1 In one minute

Most Bangladeshis who go abroad pay a dalal (দালাল, an unlicensed broker or middleman) somewhere along the way: in a 2023 national survey, 52% of migrants paid their migration costs to brokers, and 58% borrowed to go <sup>[N163 (2024)](#n163), [N72 (2024)](#n72)</sup>. Estimates of how many are cheated differ: the programme director of RMMRU (the Refugee and Migratory Movements Research Unit) said in April 2026 that research shows about 31% of people who go abroad are cheated in one way or another, at steps starting from the passport, and its executive director, citing an RMMRU study, said in July 2026 that about 51% are cheated, 19% paid and still could not go, and 32% were tortured after going <sup>[N64](#n64), [N81](#n81)</sup>. An ILO (International Labour Organization) consultant says the dalal is often a friend or relative, because people trust them with large sums more than strangers <sup>[N163 (2024)](#n163)</sup>. Behind the village dalal sit more middlemen, a licensed agency and, abroad, a sponsor or broker who sells the visa itself; one agency owner counted five to six levels of brokers <sup>[N164 (2018)](#n164), [N34](#n34)</sup>. Each scam below works by selling something that is not what it seems: a visa with no job behind it, a tourist visa called a work visa, a contract that changes on arrival, a medical or skill-test "pass" for money, a fake clearance card, a European work permit that was never issued, or a marriage that is really trafficking <sup>[N34](#n34), [N165](#n165), [N95](#n95), [64](#o64), [N166](#n166), [N167](#n167), [126](#o126)</sup>. Getting the money back is hard: in the 2026 Cambodia cases the CID (Criminal Investigation Department of the police) said many returnees were reluctant to go to law and others bargained with brokers, and complaints without documents are rejected <sup>[N166](#n166), [N168 (2019)](#n168), [N169 (2024)](#n169)</sup>. Taking money on a false job promise or holding someone's passport is a crime for anyone, not only agencies, with up to 5 years in prison, and trafficking carries at least 5 years up to life <sup>[26](#o26), [127 (2013)](#o127), [126](#o126)</sup>. Before paying, the official checks are free: BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training, the government body that licenses agencies and clears workers) checks a job visa or demand letter in 3 working days, and Saudi Arabia, Singapore and other destinations let you check a visa or permit online <sup>[10](#o10), [128](#o128), [129](#o129)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which RMMRU studies give the 31% and the 51% cheated figures, from which year and sample, and which is the current one?

<a id="who-a-dalal-is"></a>

### 2.2 Who a dalal is

The word dalal means broker <sup>[N170](#n170)</sup>. Since 2023 the law recognises a "sub-agent", a person registered with BMET who collects workers for a licensed agency; anyone else who does that work, or claims to, commits a crime <sup>[44 (2023)](#o44), [26](#o26)</sup>. A 2024 study of Bangladeshi migrants named recruitment through a sub-agent or dalal among the top risk factors they face <sup>[N171 (2024)](#n171)</sup>. A Prothom Alo guide of July 2025 said dalals usually press people by promising to get the work done quickly, do not show proper papers, hide their own identity, and deceive with false visas, false job offers and demands for extra money <sup>[N74](#n74)</sup>.

<a id="why-people-go-through-a-dalal"></a>

#### 2.2.1 Why people go through a dalal

- **No agency nearby.** Because recruiting agencies are concentrated in Dhaka, dalals play the important role in the field, the director of WARBE Development Foundation said at a Cumilla roundtable in April 2026 <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>. The associate director of BRAC's Migration Programme said in August 2026 that there is no agency in Sylhet, Tangail, Munshiganj or the other districts from which most people go, so people there reach Dhaka through middlemen; because of middlemen at home and abroad and workers' lack of skills, he said, Bangladeshi workers' cost of going abroad is the highest in the world and their income comparatively low <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>. A December 2025 Prothom Alo column said there are very few legal recruiting agencies in the Rangpur region, so people there cannot even learn how going abroad works, and named lack of agencies, lack of a migrant network, inability to raise several lakh taka and fear of dalal fraud among the reasons few people go abroad from Rangpur division <sup>[N56](#n56)</sup>.
- **One stop for everything.** Cumilla's additional deputy commissioner said in April 2026 that migrants have long demanded a one-stop service, and that because the government has not fully started one, many turn to dalals, who handle the whole process from start to finish; the person often does not know how or at what cost he is going <sup>[N109](#n109), [N64](#n64)</sup>.
- **No other source of information.** An IOM (International Organization for Migration) officer said in May 2026 that for lack of correct, reliable information people at village level depend on dalals for it, and that a pilot funded by the Italian government delivers information through Union Digital Centres (UDCs, the government's grassroots service centres) <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>. A former secretary general of BAIRA (the Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies) said in August 2026 that dependence on brokers would fall if the list of legal recruiting agencies and correct information about jobs reached upazila level <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.
- **Who relies on them.** Dr Selim Reza, coordinator of the Centre for Migration Studies at North South University, wrote in September 2026 that recent studies show a large share of people waiting to go abroad have not finished secondary education and most rely on relatives, local brokers, sub-agents and informal networks instead of licensed agencies, adding that these indicate a trend and are not exact national statistics; by government figures, he wrote, 8 to 9 lakh of the roughly 22 to 23 lakh young people entering the labour market each year have to look for work abroad <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>. Studies of Bangladeshi workers in the Maldives by RMMRU with the Asia Foundation found that 96% had gone through relatives or dalals, and that an irregular worker there works for lower pay and sends home more than Tk 2 lakh a year, against more than Tk 3 lakh for a legal one, Prothom Alo reported in May 2024 <sup>[N172 (2024)](#n172)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - In which unions does the IOM-backed UDC information pilot run, and what can a would-be migrant get there?

<a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller"></a>

#### 2.2.2 The chain from the village to the visa seller

1. <a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller-item-1"></a>**The visa seller abroad.** In Saudi Arabia, a Bangladeshi agent in the trade told Bonik Barta in 2026 that sponsors (the employer or household that legally owns a worker's visa) take out work permits for workers they do not need and sell them; the worker enters as that sponsor's employee but then works for another company <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>. In Kuwait, brokers lure Kuwaiti citizens into obtaining worker visas, and a visa that costs KWD 6 (Kuwaiti dinars) in processing and attestation fees is sold for KWD 2,000-2,200 (about Tk 8-9 lakh), though the employer also spends about KWD 250 on the work permit and other costs <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>. The joint general secretary of the Bangladesh Press Club in Kuwait said in July 2025 that an organised broker ring there controls the visa trade, which is why Bangladeshis pay more than other South Asians <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>.
2. <a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller-item-2"></a>**The licensed recruiting agency in Dhaka.** It may take workers only through registered sub-agents, from BMET's database or directly <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>. In reviews from 835 Bangladeshi migrants on the ITUC's (International Trade Union Confederation) Recruitment Advisor, 78% had relied on a sub-agent to reach the agency <sup>[N173 (2023)](#n173)</sup>. The agency completes the visa process, but collecting workers and other important work is done through dalals, the Asia Foundation's deputy team leader said in April 2026 <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>. RMMRU says Bangladesh once had 850 to 900 agencies and was taken to nearly 2,400 under the last Awami League government, against 192 in India, 464 in Pakistan, 416 in Nepal and 248 in Sri Lanka; the interim government approved 252 new licences in 2025, and 2,646 agencies in all have taken licences, Prothom Alo reported in September 2026 <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>. The general secretary of the Bangladesh Ovibashi Mohila Sramik Association (an association of women migrant workers) said in May 2026 that of about 2,200 agencies 1,100 are valid, and that ethical recruitment has still not become real <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>. Selim Reza wrote in May 2026 that many people, from village-level middlemen to licensed recruiting agencies, are part of the broker ring that controls the sector, and that overcharging, false promises, fake contract papers and supplying illegal visas have become a "normal" business process <sup>[N174](#n174)</sup>. BAIRA's administrator said in August 2026 that not all recruiting businessmen are dishonest and many send workers by the rules, while some fraud rings spread their nets in remote areas and take people's money <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.
3. <a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller-item-3"></a>**Layers of middlemen.** A visa passes through six or seven hands before reaching the worker, according to TIB (Transparency International Bangladesh) <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>. One agency owner said that with five to six levels of brokers, agencies cannot control the cost <sup>[N164 (2018)](#n164)</sup>. A Bangladeshi worker in Riyadh wrote in August 2025 that he learned only after reaching Saudi Arabia that his visa had passed through six dalals' hands, that he had no way of knowing this in Bangladesh, and that after finding no work he spoke to five dalals (see [Contract substitution: a different job or wage on arrival](#contract-substitution-a-different-job-or-wage-on-arrival)) <sup>[N124](#n124)</sup>. In Sylhet the middleman is often a travel agency: the assistant director of the DEMO (District Employment and Manpower Office, BMET's district office) in Sylhet said in April 2026 that the about 2,900 licensed agencies are all Dhaka-centred, only a few work directly in Sylhet and the rest work as brokers under the cover of travel agencies, so money beyond the set limit is taken <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>. The general secretary of the Sylhet District Press Club said the same month, without giving a source, that Sylhet has over 150 licensed travel agencies but more than 500 work illegally, taking Tk 8 lakh to 20 lakh to send people abroad <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>.
4. <a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller-item-4"></a>**The village dalal.** This is often a friend or relative, according to an ILO consultant <sup>[N163 (2024)](#n163)</sup>. In one 2025 Cambodia case the first meeting was at a tea stall across the highway from Dhaka airport; the man paid Tk 1 lakh to the dalal and the rest to a manpower agency in Fakirapool, Dhaka <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>.
5. <a id="the-chain-from-the-village-to-the-visa-seller-item-5"></a>**The online dalal.** Many dalals now work through Facebook pages and WhatsApp, with no signboard, no trade licence and sometimes no real name; they call themselves "recruitment consultant", "visa processing expert" or a foreign company's "official representative" <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>.

The minister for expatriates' welfare said in August 2026 that Dubai is taking 6,000 drivers and that the ministry is arranging for Dubai's representatives to come to Bangladesh and recruit directly once satisfied with the training, which he said would leave middlemen no scope for fraud <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - The 2026 counts of licensed agencies range from about 2,200 to about 2,900: how many hold a valid licence now, and on which BMET page is the current list?
> - Has the Dubai drivers' direct recruitment started, and where does a driver apply?

<a id="the-commonest-abuses"></a>

#### 2.2.3 The commonest abuses

- **In the trade's own words.** Farzana Alam Majumder, a sub-agent and BAIRA member from Cumilla, said at the April 2026 Cumilla roundtable that the main problems are unlicensed dalals, false promises, cheating in cash transactions, fraud and keeping the contract paper hidden <sup>[N109](#n109), [N64](#n64)</sup>.
- **What police see.** Cumilla's additional superintendent of police (Sadar Circle) said at the same roundtable that many people go abroad through dalals without a valid contract and later become undocumented, that complaints include not getting fair work or pay and even being held, and that action is hard because the necessary papers are missing <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>.
- **Who is overcharged.** The assistant director of the Cumilla DEMO said there that agencies do not fully follow the policy and in many cases take extra money from unskilled or uneducated people <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>.

<a id="the-law-on-sub-agents"></a>

#### 2.2.4 The law on sub-agents

- **Only registered sub-agents are legal.** Since the Overseas Employment and Migrants (Amendment) Act 2023, an agency may appoint only a registered person as its sub-agent or representative, and BMET issues the registration <sup>[45 (2023)](#o45), [130](#o130)</sup>. Under section 35 of the Overseas Employment and Migrants Act 2013 (the OEM Act), an agency that appoints an unregistered one faces 1 year's prison or a Tk 1-5 lakh fine, and the unregistered dalal faces 1 year or a Tk 50,000 to 2 lakh fine <sup>[44 (2023)](#o44), [26](#o26)</sup>.
- **What a registered sub-agent must show.** He must run an office with a signboard giving his name, registration number and date, the agency's name and licence number, and his addresses and phone numbers, and must display his registration certificate <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>. BMET issues the certificate, and BMET or the DEMO publishes sub-agents' details on its website <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.
- **Where the list should be.** Each agency must publish its list of registered sub-agents on its own website, and may take workers only through registered sub-agents, from BMET's database or directly <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.
- **Who pays if he cheats.** Under the 2025 licence and sub-agent rules, the agency and its sub-agent are each and jointly liable for whatever the sub-agent does <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>. Each sub-agent lodges Tk 3 lakh security with the agency by pay order, and a worker cheated by him is compensated from it once the irregularity is proved <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>. BMET's Director General can suspend or cancel a sub-agent's registration, after an inquiry and hearing, if a complaint by a worker, a relative or a well-wisher is proved <sup>[130](#o130)</sup>.
- **Not yet working in practice.** The Asia Foundation's deputy team leader said in April 2026 that rules followed the 2023 amendment in January 2025, but there has been little progress in registering dalals and bringing their work inside a structure <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>. A senior ILO programme manager said in May 2026 that the 2025 rules clearly set out agencies' and sub-agents' responsibility and accountability, but effective enforcement has not yet begun <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>. RMMRU's programme director said the same month that RMMRU helped the government frame the rules that provided for sub-agent registration, that this is still not fully implemented, and that RMMRU also worked on alternative dispute resolution to make BMET's arbitration more transparent <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>. The principal of the Cumilla Technical Training Centre said in April 2026 that a clear decision is needed on which body (the ministry, BMET or another) regulates sub-agents and middlemen, and that with no defined structure they often take advantage <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>. The sub-agent Farzana Alam Majumder recommended in April 2026 that BMET bring all sub-agents under registration and that the ministry frame rules for the system <sup>[N109](#n109)</sup>.
- **Speakers who say there is no law.** At a Prothom Alo and Democracy International roundtable in Cumilla on 11 April 2026, RMMRU's programme director attributed the 31% of migrants cheated to the lack of a law for intermediaries or sub-agents, and the administrator of Cumilla City Corporation said most people cheated abroad are cheated because of intermediaries, that Bangladesh has no law for intermediaries in migration, and that one is needed at once <sup>[N109](#n109)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Has BMET registered any sub-agents yet, and where exactly is the public list (which page of bmet.gov.bd or OEP)?
> - Do agencies actually publish their sub-agent lists on their websites, and how many do?
> - Speakers in 2026 say there is no law for intermediaries, though the 2023 amendment and the 2025 rules provide for sub-agent registration: is section 35 being enforced against unregistered dalals, and has anyone been convicted under it?

<a id="what-people-pay-and-to-whom"></a>

#### 2.2.5 What people pay, and to whom

- **The average bill.** A 2020 survey by the Bangladesh Bureau of Statistics with the ILO found a worker spends Tk 416,789 on average to go abroad <sup>[N175](#n175)</sup>. An OKUP (Ovibashi Karmi Unnayan Program, a migrant workers' organisation) survey put spending at five times the government-set cost on average <sup>[N176](#n176)</sup>. The acting regional head of Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB, the state bank for migrants) in Sylhet said in April 2026 that by the ministry's list going through the government channel costs about Tk 1.6-1.7 lakh, but most people in practice spend more than Tk 4-5 lakh, and that agencies' profit reaching Tk 2.5-3 lakh cannot be justified <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>.
- **To whom.** In the 2023 survey, 52.03% of migrants paid their migration costs to brokers <sup>[N163 (2024)](#n163)</sup>. Broker use was highest in Sylhet (58.53%) and Mymensingh (57.44%) divisions and lowest in Barishal (28.83%) <sup>[N163 (2024)](#n163)</sup>.
- **Example of the split.** In the 2025 Cambodia case above, the dalal asked Tk 7 lakh in all for a job he said would pay Tk 80,000 a month; the man paid Tk 1 lakh to the dalal and the rest to the agency <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>. A December 2025 Prothom Alo column estimated a Saudi pest-control job at about Tk 5.7 lakh: about Tk 4.6 lakh to the recruiting agency as its fee, about Tk 60,000 for the passport, medical test, Takamol certificate, PDO (pre-departure orientation) training and police clearance, and roughly Tk 50,000 to sub-agents at various levels <sup>[N56](#n56)</sup>.
- **The official figure.** The ministry fixed the official migration cost for Saudi Arabia in 2016 at Tk 165,000 <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>. In one BMET permission letter for Saudi kitchen workers, the agency could take at most Tk 50,000 per worker <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What does a village dalal keep per worker on a typical Saudi or Malaysia visa in 2026, and what does the sub-agent above him keep?

<a id="fake-visa-demand-letter-or-job-offer"></a>

### 2.3 Fake visa, demand letter or job offer

A demand letter is the employer's written request for workers, attested by a Bangladesh mission abroad <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>.

**How it works:**

1. A dalal or unlicensed office shows a visa, demand letter or job offer, takes the passport and money, and the worker is sent to the airport or never hears again <sup>[N177 (2020)](#n177), [N178 (2022)](#n178)</sup>.
2. Some papers are entirely forged: one Dhaka ring printed Oman visas on a computer and supplied fake tickets and fake BMET clearance <sup>[N178 (2022)](#n178)</sup>. Another office held fake Fiji demand letters and fake contracts <sup>[N177 (2020)](#n177)</sup>. A broker in Fulbaria, Mymensingh, made fake tickets in the names of Jazeera Airways, Air Arabia, Salam Air, Egypt Air and other airlines, according to RAB (the Rapid Action Battalion, an elite police unit) <sup>[N179](#n179)</sup>.
3. Some rings use local collectors: according to the CID (the Criminal Investigation Department of the police), Milon Mia of Faridpur, an earlier-arrested member of the ring led by Josna Khatun (see the 23 Sept 2025 case below), collected people from different areas in the name of sending them abroad, and the ring took several crore taka from at least a hundred people <sup>[N180](#n180)</sup>.
4. Some are real papers that lead nowhere: a broker told Bonik Barta in 2024 that some Saudi citizens issue visas under shell companies, and brokers disappear once paid <sup>[N181 (2024)](#n181)</sup>. A former BAIRA (Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies) official said in 2026 that some Saudi visas first appear in the online system and later disappear, and industry insiders said the Saudi labour ministry had cancelled several thousand applications filed on fake demand letters <sup>[N127](#n127)</sup>.
5. An attested demand letter is not proof: TIB found some Bangladeshi mission officials attest demand letters without checks or for bribes <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>.
6. Small demand letters slipped through: until 4 Nov 2026 a demand letter for up to seven workers needed no permission from the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, people concerned allege this was used to bring in fake appointment letters, and since that date every demand letter must be approved first <sup>[N182](#n182)</sup>.
7. Contracts with fake employers: a senior officer at the Sandwip branch of Probashi Kallyan Bank (PKB, the state migrant bank) said in May 2026 that in many cases they see fraudulent contracts between a fake employer and the worker, and that many people still sell their homestead to go abroad and end up in trouble <sup>[N100](#n100)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- an office with no recruiting licence that advertises online and collects passports <sup>[N177 (2020)](#n177)</sup>;
- a Saudi visa whose company cannot be found in the Ministry of Commerce's commercial register (Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 1: Find a real job, Before you pay anyone) <sup>[131](#o131)</sup>;
- for a UAE job, any of the five signs of a fake offer named by MOHRE (the UAE Ministry of Human Resources and Emiratisation) <sup>[N183](#n183)</sup>:
  - being asked to open a new bank account in your own name as part of recruitment <sup>[N183](#n183)</sup>;
  - being asked for confidential bank account details by an unverified person or company <sup>[N183](#n183)</sup>;
  - a demand for money to confirm the job or complete recruitment <sup>[N183](#n183)</sup>;
  - being asked to receive money into your account or transfer it on to another account <sup>[N183](#n183)</sup>;
  - contact only through WhatsApp or another messaging app, with no institutional phone number or address <sup>[N183](#n183)</sup>.

MOHRE says that anyone who meets these signs after a job offer should stop the process at once, verify the offer, and give no money or personal information until the offer letter is confirmed genuine <sup>[N183](#n183)</sup>.

**Check before paying:**

1. <a id="fake-visa-demand-letter-or-job-offer-item-1"></a>**At BMET or your DEMO:** take a copy of the visa or demand letter (and, where relevant, the passport page with the visa) to BMET, 89/2 Kakrail, Dhaka-1000, or your DEMO; the check is free and takes 3 working days <sup>[10](#o10), [132](#o132)</sup>. BMET checks Saudi, Qatar, Bahrain, Singapore and UAE visas online, and its visa-check app is on Google Play <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
2. <a id="fake-visa-demand-letter-or-job-offer-item-2"></a>**On the destination's own system:**
   - Saudi Arabia: KSA Visa Track, with the visa or application number and your passport number (Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 7: Check the visa yourself) <sup>[128](#o128)</sup>.
   - Malaysia: MyIMMs application status, with the employer's registration number and application number (Malaysia doc: Work visa: company worker (PLKS), Step 5: Check the visa yourself) <sup>[133](#o133)</sup>.
   - Qatar: the Interior Ministry's Visa Inquiry (Qatar doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 5: Check the visa yourself) <sup>[134](#o134)</sup>.
   - UAE: the job offer, by its reference number, on MOHRE's enquiry service, inquiry.mohre.gov.ae (UAE doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 5: Check your visa and permit are real) <sup>[124](#o124)</sup>. Every valid offer issued through MOHRE's system carries a specific serial number and a barcode, and it can also be checked in the "MOHRE UAE" mobile app or by calling 600590000 <sup>[N183](#n183)</sup>.
   - Singapore: the "Check work pass and application status" service of MOM (the Ministry of Manpower), no login needed (Singapore doc: Work visa: Work Permit (construction, marine shipyard, process and other sectors), Step 5: The employer applies; check your IPA) <sup>[129](#o129)</sup>.
   - Maldives: Work Permit Verification on xpat.egov.mv (Maldives doc: Work visa: company worker (construction, resort, shop), Step 5: Check your permit and visa are real) <sup>[135](#o135)</sup>.
   - Kyrgyzstan: Check status on the e-visa portal with the reference number (Kyrgyzstan doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 5: The work visa, and checking it is real) <sup>[136](#o136)</sup>.
   - Oman: the visa status shows only in the sponsor's own account, so only the employer can check it (Oman doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 6: The employer gets the employment visa from the ROP; check it) <sup>[137](#o137)</sup>.
   - Kuwait: BMET's check covers the visa and demand letter (Kuwait doc: Work visa: private-sector worker (Article 18), Step 5: Medical check and checking your visa is real) <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- 15 Sept 2020: a RAB mobile court found that "Dream Visa", Road 4, Banani, had no recruiting licence, seized 32 passports, fake contracts and fake Fiji demand letters, and jailed the owner for six months after he admitted fraud <sup>[N177 (2020)](#n177)</sup>.
- Aug 2022: RAB arrested members of an unlicensed Dhaka ring that, according to RAB, took Tk 4 lakh each and supplied fake Oman visas, fake tickets and fake BMET clearance; victims were turned back at the airport <sup>[N178 (2022)](#n178)</sup>.
- 6 June 2023 (reported May 2024): Lailul Haque, 34, of Kafura village, Sherpur upazila, Bogura, said Abdul Alim, a local businessman who sends people abroad, sent him to Malaysia on a fake visa, and he was sent back to Bangladesh after six days in jail <sup>[N184 (2024)](#n184)</sup>. A Sherpur police sub-inspector said Alim took Tk 5 lakh 70 thousand from him and Tk 2 lakh 30 thousand, Tk 3 lakh 14 thousand, Tk 2 lakh 70 thousand and Tk 3 lakh 50 thousand from four other young men; Lailul put the total for the five at Tk 17 lakh 34 thousand <sup>[N184 (2024)](#n184)</sup>.
- 7 Apr 2024: the CID said Jannat Trade International took Tk 5 lakh 12 thousand each from three people with a promise of well-paid jobs in Kyrgyzstan and gave them so-called Kyrgyz visas and air tickets; at Hazrat Shahjalal International Airport, immigration police found no record of the visas online and stopped them <sup>[N185 (2024)](#n185)</sup>. A case was filed at Paltan police station, Dhaka, under the Prevention and Suppression of Human Trafficking Act, and the CID arrested three people, including the proprietor, Ibrahim Mallik Nahid, whom it called the head of the ring <sup>[N185 (2024)](#n185)</sup>.
- 18 Feb 2025: Abdul Gani, 30, of Shushuti village, Fulbaria, Mymensingh, said he had paid a local manpower broker, Shafiqul Islam, Tk 6 lakh 10 thousand to go to Turkey; Shafiqul kept his passport and money for a year and a half, then said Turkey was not possible and he would go to Saudi Arabia within 20 days, and handed him a "Jazeera Airways" ticket and a visa that both turned out to be fake <sup>[N179](#n179)</sup>. Local residents alleged Shafiqul had taken nearly Tk 1 crore in a few years on promises of jobs abroad, and the two complainants said many people nearby had been cheated through him <sup>[N179](#n179)</sup>. RAB-14 detained him in Gazipur on 29 May 2025 and handed him to Fulbaria police; RAB said he had been cheating village people for about three years and a case would be filed under the regular law, and the officer-in-charge said the complaint would be verified <sup>[N179](#n179)</sup>.
- 23 Sept 2025: the CID arrested Josna Khatun, 35, of Narail, named main accused in a fraud case at Paltan police station; the CID said in a press release that the ring lured people with jobs abroad, took large sums through several bank accounts and gave them fake visas <sup>[N180](#n180)</sup>. According to Prothom Alo's report, the ring promised high-paid jobs in Italy, also took money on promises of important government posts, and its network covers Faridpur, Narail, Narsingdi, Kishoreganj, Chandpur, Chattogram and other districts <sup>[N180](#n180)</sup>.

- Reported Sept 2026: Selim Reza wrote that, as far as he and his colleagues had learned, thousands of Bangladeshis were cheated over the past five years after paying large sums for promised factory jobs in Malaysia <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>.

**What to do:** taking money on a false job promise is an offence under section 31 of the OEM Act (see [File a police case](#file-a-police-case)) <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>; complain to BMET too (see [Complain to BMET](#complain-to-bmet)) <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>. The 2024 Kyrgyzstan case above was filed under the Prevention and Suppression of Human Trafficking Act (see [File a trafficking case](#file-a-trafficking-case)) <sup>[N185 (2024)](#n185)</sup>.

<a id="visa-trading-and-the-free-visa"></a>

### 2.4 Visa trading and the "free visa"

A "free visa" (ফ্রি ভিসা) is a real visa bought from a sponsor who has no job for you <sup>[N34](#n34), [N186 (2020)](#n186)</sup>.

**How it works:**

1. A sponsor abroad takes out a visa in its own name and sells it through brokers <sup>[N34](#n34), [N39](#n39)</sup>.
2. Dalals sell it in Bangladesh, though it gives only permission to enter, with no job guaranteed <sup>[N176](#n176)</sup>.
3. On arrival in Saudi Arabia you are the sponsor's employee in name only: you must find your own work and housing, may pay the sponsor a monthly cut, and can be deported for breaking your visa's conditions <sup>[N186 (2020)](#n186)</sup>.
4. The visa may belong to a "supply company" (a labour-supply firm): a worker in Riyadh wrote in August 2025 that he later learned his visa and the villa he was housed in both belonged to one, and by his description such a company sells workers on to other places at a high price for its own profit, and the worker placed there is not paid <sup>[N124](#n124)</sup>.

**What dalals say:** "free visa" (ফ্রি ভিসা), "easy job" (সহজ চাকরি), "quick departure" (কম সময়ে যাত্রা) <sup>[N176](#n176)</sup>. In Malaysia the same trick is sold as a "calling visa" <sup>[N187](#n187)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- the words "free visa": BMET says there is no such thing <sup>[132](#o132)</sup>, and a Prothom Alo editorial of October 2025 said that under ILO conventions there is no free visa, only a fraudulent arrangement in which the worker is given permission to enter, or a sponsorship is sold, with no specific job arranged <sup>[N188](#n188)</sup>;
- a household named as the employer on a work visa, with other signs listed in Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, Avoid a "free visa" <sup>[N186 (2020)](#n186)</sup>;
- a price far above the official cost: a Kuwait visa that costs KWD 6 in fees is sold for Tk 8-9 lakh <sup>[N39](#n39)</sup>.

**Check before paying:** BMET says to have any so-called free visa and its papers checked at BMET in Dhaka or the DEMO, and to confirm the exact job, pay and benefits <sup>[132](#o132)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- Reported July 2024: a Pirojpur man sold his business, mortgaged land and borrowed heavily to go to Oman on a free visa through a dalal, and got no work for a year <sup>[N189 (2024)](#n189)</sup>.
- Reported Jan 2026: many who went to Malaysia on a calling visa or free visa found no work on arrival <sup>[N187](#n187)</sup>.
- Reported Sept 2026: hundreds of people from Munshiganj and Cumilla paid about Tk 4-6 lakh each after Facebook ads for Saudi "free visa" jobs, and many found the visa was not a work permit <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>.

**What to do:** obtaining a demand letter, visa or work permit by illegal means and buying or selling it inside Bangladesh is an offence under section 33 of the OEM Act, with 2 to 7 years' rigorous imprisonment and a fine of at least Tk 3 lakh <sup>[138](#o138), [26](#o26)</sup>. The government legal aid helpline is 16699 (see [Free legal help](#free-legal-help)) <sup>[139](#o139)</sup>.

<a id="a-visit-or-tourist-visa-sold-as-a-job"></a>

### 2.5 A visit or tourist visa sold as a job

**How it works:**

1. The dalal promises a work visa but the passport carries a tourist or visit visa <sup>[N165](#n165), [N181 (2024)](#n181)</sup>.
2. The worker enters legally, the visa runs out, and the worker is illegal with no job or employer <sup>[N181 (2024)](#n181)</sup>.
3. Full papers are no protection: Biswajit Bhowmik, an advocate with BNWLA (the Bangladesh National Women Lawyers' Association), said in June 2026 that many people from Cox's Bazar fly with a passport, visa and BMET card but are sent on 90-day visas, and after arriving get neither permission to stay permanently nor any work and are forced to come home empty-handed <sup>[N99](#n99)</sup>.
4. Some travellers are coached: a woman taken to Malaysia on a visit visa in November 2025 said she had been taught beforehand what to say at immigration <sup>[N190](#n190)</sup>.

**What dalals say:** that a one- or two-month tourist visa is a two-year work visa <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>, or that a 3-month tourist visa will become a 15-month iqama (Saudi residence permit) <sup>[N181 (2024)](#n181)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- a job offered on a tourist or visit visa: the ministry warns not to go to work on one on a broker's promise <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>;
- destinations in the ministry's 5 Feb 2025 warning: the Middle East, Russia, Greece, Libya, Lebanon, Egypt, Cambodia and Malaysia <sup>[140](#o140)</sup>.

**Check before paying:** the visa type shows in the destination's own visa check (Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 7: Check the visa yourself) <sup>[128](#o128)</sup>, and BMET's free check says whether it is a work visa <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- Apr 2023 to May 2026: a Keraniganj woman, then 20, promised a Tk 50,000-a-month restaurant job in Dubai, was given a visit visa, had her passport taken on arrival and was made to work in a dance bar for three years, she told Samakal <sup>[N191](#n191)</sup>.
- 15 Nov 2025: Naznin Akter was taken to Malaysia on a visit visa for a promised restaurant job, having been taught what to say at immigration <sup>[N190](#n190)</sup>. On arrival her passport, ticket and papers were taken and she was told she would get no work unless she paid the balance, although she had kept Tk 5,000 unpaid on the agreement that she would pay it after starting work, she told a roundtable at Prothom Alo in July 2026 <sup>[N190](#n190)</sup>.
- Reported July 2026: Cambodia victims travelled on one- or two-month tourist visas their brokers called two-year work visas <sup>[N165](#n165)</sup>.

**What to do:** see File a police case and, if you were forced to work, File a trafficking case <sup>[26](#o26), [126](#o126)</sup>.

<a id="contract-substitution-a-different-job-or-wage-on-arrival"></a>

### 2.6 Contract substitution: a different job or wage on arrival

**How it works:**

1. The contract shown in Bangladesh names one employer, job and wage <sup>[N34](#n34)</sup>.
2. On arrival the worker signs a new contract at the real, lower wage, or is sent to a different employer <sup>[N47 (2024)](#n47), [N34](#n34)</sup>.

**What dalals say:** that the job title on the visa is temporary and will change once the worker proves himself <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>. In a 2025 Saudi case, asked directly whether the job was really at a chocolate factory, the seller said to have faith and not worry <sup>[N124](#n124)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- a job title on the visa that differs from the job promised: in a 2025 Cambodia case the visa named a construction job and the broker called it temporary <sup>[N166](#n166)</sup>;
- no written contract: Cumilla's additional superintendent of police said in April 2026 that many people go through dalals without a valid contract, later become undocumented, and complain of unfair work or pay, and that action is hard because the papers are missing <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>.

**Check before paying:** compare the employer, job and salary on the destination's record with what you were promised. Singapore's IPA (In-Principle Approval letter) states the job and salary, and MOM's online check shows Work Permit holders their salary information (Singapore doc: Work visa: Work Permit (construction, marine shipyard, process and other sectors), Step 5: The employer applies; check your IPA) <sup>[N192](#n192), [129](#o129)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- Until 2020 (reported Oct 2024): Islam Hossain of Dumuria upazila, speaking at a workshop in Khulna, said that in Oman he was not given the wage and work promised and was made to work 12 to 15 hours; he stayed five years and came home in 2020 during the Covid pandemic <sup>[N193 (2024)](#n193)</sup>.
- 2020 (reported Oct 2024): Md Shanto Khan of Jamira village, Phultala, Khulna, paid Tk 4.2 lakh to go to Saudi Arabia through a dalal to work as a cleaner in a bank; he said he was put to work at a petrol pump and then in a date garden and did not get the wage promised <sup>[N193 (2024)](#n193)</sup>. He fell ill within two years, came home in mid-2023 by what he described as more or less running away, and in October 2024 the bank loan his father took and the money borrowed from relatives were still unpaid <sup>[N193 (2024)](#n193)</sup>.
- Oct 2022 (reported Sept 2024): a Narsingdi man who paid a broker Tk 4 lakh for a Saudi pharmacy job with a 15-month iqama got a restaurant cleaner's job with a 3-month iqama, and became undocumented <sup>[N181 (2024)](#n181)</sup>.
- Nov 2023 (reported May 2024): Nayem Uddin of Narsingdi went to Malaysia through a broker on Tk 5.2 lakh borrowed at interest; promised a tiles-factory job, he was given construction work for a company that stopped his work after 15 days, kept him in an office for another two and a half months and then sent him home, where he could not pay the loan instalments <sup>[N194 (2024)](#n194)</sup>.
- July 2024 (reported Feb 2026): a Bangladeshi paid more than EUR 10,000 for a job with a Romanian transport company; on arrival there was no sign of the company, and he was sent to a cement manufacturer <sup>[N195](#n195)</sup>.
- Reported Aug 2025: a Bangladeshi worker in Riyadh wrote that the man who sent him was from the next village, had lived in Saudi Arabia about five years and was known locally as polite and decent; he fixed the visa price at Tk 4 lakh and promised work at a good chocolate company <sup>[N124](#n124)</sup>. A driver took the group from King Khalid airport to a company room in Malaz, Riyadh; the worker bought his own food for a month with no work, the man who sent him, living far away near the Yemen border, put him off for two months with "today or tomorrow", and there was no chocolate factory <sup>[N124](#n124)</sup>.
- Reported Apr 2026: Fatema Akter, a returnee, told a Cumilla roundtable she worked in Oman for five years on a salary of only Tk 12,000, then two years in Saudi Arabia, where for some of the time she received a salary of about Tk 3,000; she said food was not given properly, wages were irregular, she was made to work all the time, and she came home ill <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>.
- Apr-May 2026: an overseas agent sent a Singapore-bound worker from India a screenshot of an "approved" pass, took about S$8,000, then sent an IPA with a different job and lower salary <sup>[N192](#n192)</sup>.
- 4 Oct 2026: the Attorney General said Bangladesh's labour market in Malaysia collapsed because of money taken beyond the fixed fees, people promised one job and given another, and fake visas, and that cases were filed against those involved under the interim government and charge sheets submitted after investigation <sup>[N65](#n65)</sup>.
- Nov 2026: the minister told Parliament that complaints of wages below the contract are among those being investigated <sup>[N196](#n196)</sup>.

**What to do:** being sent to a different job is grounds for a BMET complaint (see [Complain to BMET](#complain-to-bmet)) <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>; luring someone with false promises of higher pay is an offence under section 31 of the OEM Act <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.

<a id="the-staged-payment-trap-and-no-receipts"></a>

### 2.7 The staged-payment trap, and no receipts

**How it works:**

1. The dalal takes money in instalments, each tied to a promised step: "processing", the visa, the medical, the ticket <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>.
2. The passport may be kept until the last instalment <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>.
3. Money goes in cash or by mobile money, with no receipt <sup>[N73](#n73), [N169 (2024)](#n169)</sup>. In Malaysia recruitment in 2022-24, several BAIRA members allege that part of each Malaysia worker's cost went to Ruhul Amin, known as Swapan, part of it laundered to Malaysia through hundi, the rest to the agency in whose name the worker went, and that the money always changed hands in cash without a receipt; Ruhul Amin told Prothom Alo the allegations were false, that nothing was taken beyond the government-fixed Tk 78,990 and that no money was sent to Malaysia <sup>[N194 (2024)](#n194)</sup>.
4. When it fails, there is no proof of who was paid what <sup>[N169 (2024)](#n169)</sup>.

**What dalals say:** fees for "registration" (রেজিস্ট্রেশন), "visa processing" (ভিসা প্রসেসিং) or "document verification" (ডকুমেন্ট ভেরিফিকেশন) <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>. Police said a Nilphamari ring arrested in July 2026 took money in stages by mobile banking as "medical fee", "visa processing fee", "document verification", "ticket" and other costs, after assuring people the job was confirmed <sup>[N197](#n197)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- cash only, or payment to a personal bKash or Nagad number <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>;
- money asked before the visa, BMET clearance and flight are confirmed, which a BMET permission letter forbids <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>.

**Check before paying:** BMET says to deal with the agency through a bank and keep receipts <sup>[34](#o34), [132](#o132)</sup>. In one BMET permission letter, the agency could take money only by cheque, bank draft or pay order, and only after the visa, BMET clearance and flight were confirmed <sup>[27](#o27)</sup>. At a BRAC Migration Programme workshop in Khulna on 31 October 2024, attended by district officials and police, speakers said migrants are cheated most when they go through brokers, that many keep no proof of the money they hand over, and advised keeping proof of every transaction <sup>[N193 (2024)](#n193)</sup>. The minister said in April 2026 that the government plans, with the Bangladesh Bank governor, a "Probashi card" so that migrants are served through a set system instead of cash transactions <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- Aug 2022: a Dhaka ring gave refund cheques that bounced <sup>[N178 (2022)](#n178)</sup>.
- Reported Feb 2025: a Bangladeshi who paid about EUR 18,000 to reach Italy said the man handling his papers in Bangladesh kept his passport until the last instalment <sup>[N43](#n43)</sup>.
- Reported Aug 2025: the Riyadh worker in Contract substitution: a different job or wage on arrival wrote that he borrowed the Tk 4 lakh from relatives and paid the agency in instalments of Tk 50,000 or Tk 1 lakh, and that when he went to the agency's Dhaka office to collect his papers before the flight he was treated very badly and more money was taken on various pretexts <sup>[N124](#n124)</sup>.

**What to do:** BMET rejects complaints not backed by documents, and workers without written papers often fail to get compensation <sup>[N168 (2019)](#n168), [N169 (2024)](#n169)</sup>. Keep every message and transfer record and complain (see [Complain to BMET](#complain-to-bmet)) <sup>[141](#o141)</sup>.

<a id="the-passport-kept-by-the-dalal"></a>

### 2.8 The passport kept by the dalal

**How it works:**

1. The dalal or office collects the passport "for the visa" <sup>[N198](#n198), [N199](#n199)</sup>.
2. The passport becomes a lever: to get it back, the worker must pay more or accept the deal <sup>[N43](#n43), [N200](#n200)</sup>.
3. It can happen again on arrival: Naznin Akter said that on arriving in Malaysia in November 2025 her passport, ticket and papers were taken and she was told there would be no work until the balance was paid <sup>[N190](#n190)</sup>. A 2025 study by IIED (the International Institute for Environment and Development) counts the seizure of migrants' visas and passports abroad, with withheld wages, forced work and restricted movement, among the practices of modern slavery <sup>[N201](#n201)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- an office collecting many passports before any visa exists: in Sept 2026 a BMET mobile court seized three boxes of passports from one agency <sup>[N199](#n199)</sup>;
- a request for the passport to arrange a medical: the ministry warned in Oct 2026 not to hand over a passport or money without confirming official approval <sup>[N202](#n202)</sup>.

**Check before paying:** some steps need only the passport details, not the passport: for example, the Wafid medical slip is printed by entering the passport number and nationality <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- Reported Sept 2025: the CID said the Josna Khatun ring, which took money for jobs abroad and gave fake visas, also held on to many victims' passports to harass them <sup>[N180](#n180)</sup>.
- 15 Nov 2025: Naznin Akter told a Dhaka roundtable in July 2026 that on arrival in Malaysia her passport, ticket and papers were taken and she was told there would be no work until the balance was paid <sup>[N190](#n190)</sup>.
- July 2026: in Cambodia a broker still held workers' passports and demanded USD 500 more from each <sup>[N200](#n200)</sup>.
- 10 Sept 2026: a BMET mobile court sealed Triple A Overseas (RL-2630), fined it Tk 50,000 and seized three boxes of passports taken irregularly from workers, the ministry said, amid complaints that some agencies were collecting passports with promises of Malaysian visas <sup>[N199](#n199)</sup>. The ministry said the passports were in BMET's custody and that returning them to their owners through legal process, and legal action against the agency, were under way <sup>[N203](#n203)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How does a worker whose passport was seized in a BMET raid claim it back from BMET's custody, and at which office?

**What to do:** holding a migrant's passport, visa or papers without lawful reason is an offence under section 31(c) of the OEM Act: up to 5 years and a fine of at least Tk 1 lakh (see [File a police case](#file-a-police-case)) <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.

<a id="the-training-centre-and-skills-test-racket"></a>

### 2.9 The training-centre and skills-test racket

**How it works:**

1. Saudi-bound workers take a skill test under the SVP (Skill Verification Program) run by Takamol, for a set fee, USD 85 since early September 2026 (it was USD 50); dalals ask more, promising a pass <sup>[65](#o65), [N46](#n46), [64](#o64)</sup>.
2. Jordan garment jobs go through a government channel that is meant to be free, and cheats charge for it <sup>[N204](#n204)</sup>. A BOESL (Bangladesh Overseas Employment and Services Limited, the state recruiting company) deputy manager said in May 2026 that BOESL has sent women to Jordan's garment sector entirely free since 2010, its officers handling everything from the medical and pre-departure training to the airport, and that it tells workers each country's fixed migration cost in advance <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>.
3. Brokers wait where candidates gather: according to SRB (the Special Response Battalion), BOESL's test is held every Friday and Saturday at the Bangladesh-Korea Technical Training Centre (BKTTC), and a ring arrested in October 2026 worked mainly by targeting candidates there <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>. It promised a worker visa at low cost and a pass in the written test, took BDT 40,000 to 10 lakh depending on the case, then kept the money on various excuses <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>.
4. In Singapore, the state minister asked in July 2026 how agencies take Tk 12 to 15 lakh from a person going there, and said the Singapore authority does not take that money: six authorised training institutes charge it and, working as a syndicate, are making hundreds of crores of taka <sup>[N81](#n81)</sup>.
5. Training is not a job: SRB-4's commander said BKTTC teaches 24 trades and its certificates help in getting work abroad, but it does not send workers or arrange jobs <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>. Cumilla City Corporation's administrator said in April 2026 that some training centres give training but not a proper certificate, and promise jobs that do not happen <sup>[N64](#n64)</sup>. An ILO assessment of 40 TTCs (Technical Training Centres, BMET's training centres) found their training in many cases not fully in line with foreign labour markets' demand, an ILO programme manager said in May 2026 <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>.

**What dalals say:** that they can fix the result; one ILO study found dalals telling candidates how to identify themselves to the interviewer, for example with a nail-polish colour on a set finger <sup>[142 (2018)](#o142)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- any payment beyond the test fee (USD 85 since September 2026): BMET says paying extra cannot buy a Saudi skill-test pass <sup>[N46](#n46), [64](#o64)</sup>;
- a fee for a government-to-government job: BOESL has no agent or sub-agent and takes no cash <sup>[143](#o143)</sup>; SRB said in October 2026 that BOESL takes no fee from candidates in recruitment, and that a person with only a passport can get work abroad through its test at no cost <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>;
- a job promised in a training centre's name <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Does BOESL charge anything after final selection (by pay order), or nothing at all as SRB said in October 2026?

**Check before paying:** see Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 3: Pass the skill test, for the official booking and fee <sup>[64](#o64)</sup>. A real BOESL recruitment, its deputy manager said, starts with a government-to-government agreement or an employer giving BOESL the recruitment; labour wing officers at the Bangladesh embassy verify the employer's demand, BOESL publishes a notice, and a test is held with the employer or its local representative present, after which BOESL helps with the legal steps <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>.

**Cases:**

- 9 Oct 2026: SRB-4 arrested 12 members of a ring at Technical Mor, Dhaka, accused of taking money on promises of jobs abroad, worker visas and a guaranteed written-test pass; a mobile court sentenced seven to between seven days and three months in jail and fined five <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>. The action began with complaints from BKTTC and BOESL, after which SRB investigated quietly and raided with an executive magistrate present <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>.
- Reported 9 Oct 2026: 20 people arrested in a raid in Dhaka's Shyamoli were sentenced by a mobile court for taking Tk 5-10 lakh for Jordan garment jobs that are free through the government channel <sup>[N204](#n204)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are the 9 October 2026 raids at Technical Mor (12 arrested) and in Shyamoli (20 arrested) the same raid, and which count is right?

**What to do:** a worker caught paying for a pass has the Takamol account locked and can't go to Saudi Arabia <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>. BMET asks people to hand anyone taking money for a pass to the police (emergency number 999) <sup>[65](#o65), [144](#o144)</sup>. SRB asked anyone cheated this way to inform the authorities quickly and to verify information through government offices and approved agencies <sup>[N205](#n205)</sup>. BMET's Takamol and Japan Support Centre is at BMET Bhaban 4th floor, Kakrail, Dhaka (01901165981, 01901165982) <sup>[65](#o65)</sup>.

<a id="the-medical-slip-and-unfit-pay-to-pass"></a>

### 2.10 The medical "slip" and "unfit, pay to pass"

Wafid is the Gulf states' online booking for the pre-departure medical; people still call it GAMCA, after the system it replaced <sup>[N94](#n94), [59](#o59)</sup>. The "slip" is the appointment confirmation <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>.

**How it works:**

1. The slip costs USD 10 (standard) or USD 25 (premium) on wafid.com <sup>[60](#o60)</sup>.
2. Agencies sell a "package": in a 2025 case a Paltan agency offered Tk 10,000 standard, or Tk 20,000 with a guaranteed fit certificate <sup>[N95](#n95)</sup>.
3. Centres are alleged to declare candidates unfit, then ask for a bribe for a fit report <sup>[N96](#n96)</sup>.
4. Unauthorised centres test and issue reports without approval <sup>[N206](#n206)</sup>.

**What dalals say:** "fit guaranteed" <sup>[N95](#n95)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- any "guaranteed fit": a syndicate reportedly charged Tk 25,000-30,000 for one in 2025 <sup>[N95](#n95)</sup>;
- a total well above about Tk 8,500-10,000 for a Gulf medical, the range reported as the proper cost in 2025 <sup>[N95](#n95), [N96](#n96)</sup>; for Malaysia the ministry set Tk 8,000 in Oct 2026 <sup>[N207](#n207)</sup>;
- a centre not on the destination's approved list <sup>[N206](#n206)</sup>.

**Check before paying:** book the slip yourself and check the result with your passport number or slip number on Wafid (Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, Step 4: Pass the Wafid medical check) <sup>[59](#o59)</sup>. The Gulf country that issued the visa examines you again on arrival <sup>[61 (2021)](#o61)</sup>; in a 2025 case a man who paid an agent Tk 25,000 for a fit certificate was found unfit in Saudi Arabia and deported <sup>[N95](#n95)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the official total for the Gulf (Wafid) medical in Bangladesh in 2026, and where is it published?

**Cases:**

- 1 Oct 2026: a mobile court sealed Human Diagnostic and Medical Center, Paltan, Dhaka, which the ministry said had tested 14 Malaysia-bound people without government authorisation <sup>[N206](#n206)</sup>.
- Sept 2025: a former BAIRA official said only 2-5% of workers pay the set fee; the rest pay at least Tk 17,000-20,000 <sup>[N96](#n96)</sup>.

**What to do:** the ministry asks people to email secretary@probashi.gov.bd about illegal or unauthorised medical tests, with the date, place, names and evidence <sup>[N202](#n202)</sup>; the Gulf Health Council complaint route is in Saudi Arabia doc: Work visa: company worker, If you are declared unfit or overcharged <sup>[61 (2021)](#o61)</sup>.

<a id="fake-bmet-cards-and-fake-clearance"></a>

### 2.11 Fake BMET cards and fake clearance

BMET clearance (the "smart card" or emigration clearance) is the government's permission to leave as a worker; airport desks check it <sup>[70 (2024)](#o70)</sup>.

**How it works:** rings put fake visas in passports, then obtain BMET smart cards for them or forge the clearance itself, and send people to the airport <sup>[N167](#n167), [N178 (2022)](#n178)</sup>. Cards are issued offline by BMET and the district manpower offices, and online through Ami Probashi, a phone app run by a private organisation, with all data kept on a server at BMET's office <sup>[N208 (2024)](#n208)</sup>. In late 2023 and early 2024, rejected applications were approved through that system with a senior BMET official's login; BMET's inquiry said hackers used the app's lack of an automatic session time-out and of a one-time PIN (OTP), or other flaws, to do it <sup>[N208 (2024)](#n208)</sup>. People concerned told Prothom Alo in February 2024 that BMET's server could not be secured and ran on very old software, so fraud could not be stopped or traced; BMET was moving server management to the Bangladesh Computer Council (BCC), and two officials said fraud would stop once the move was done <sup>[N208 (2024)](#n208)</sup>.

**Red flags:**

- a BMET card or clearance supplied by the dalal or office itself <sup>[N178 (2022)](#n178)</sup>;
- a "clearance" for a country where the ministry has stopped clearances without its approval: since 4 Nov 2026 these include Serbia, Moldova, Belarus, Congo, Russia, Japan, Iraq, Laos, Vietnam, Cambodia, Ukraine and Libya <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>.

**Check before paying:** whether a BMET emigration clearance or card exists can be checked on the page of the WEWB (Wage Earners' Welfare Board), portal.wewb.gov.bd/bmet-wewbm-check <sup>[69](#o69)</sup>.
