> - Do airlines refund the ticket of a passenger offloaded by immigration, and on what terms?
> - Does the Ministry of Home Affairs take offloading complaints directly, or only through the GRS?
> - Which mobile number answers for OC Immigration at Dhaka airport: 01320005902 (SB contact page) or 01713373073 (SB complaint page)?

<a id="refused-entry-at-the-destination"></a>

### 3.7 Refused entry at the destination

Destinations also turn Bangladeshis back on arrival. Over 3,500 were refused entry and sent home in January to April 2025 <sup>[N320](#n320)</sup>. Border officers flag visitors with little money, unclear plans or conflicting papers <sup>[N320](#n320)</sup>.

- **Malaysia (KLIA, Kuala Lumpur International Airport).** Immigration refused 80 Bangladeshis with valid tourist visas in July 2025 for lacking "satisfactory justification", put 123 on the "Not to Land" list the same month, and refused 204 on 13 August and 98 on 15 August 2025 <sup>[N340](#n340), [N341](#n341), [N320](#n320), [N342](#n342)</sup>. Reasons given were fake hotel bookings, no return ticket, too little money and no clear purpose <sup>[N342](#n342), [N343](#n343)</sup>. Travellers are told to carry RM500 to 1,000, and officers may look at photos on phones <sup>[N344](#n344)</sup>. More than 200 refused at KLIA were deported in 2025 <sup>[N321](#n321)</sup>. In 2024 a "counter setting" syndicate was reported to sell entry through bribed officers at RM200 to 2,500 a head; Malaysia's anti-corruption agency arrested 60 people, including 49 immigration officers <sup>[N345 (2024)](#n345)</sup>.
- **UAE.** The UAE tightened visitor and employment visas for Bangladeshis from July 2024 <sup>[N320](#n320)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshis did Saudi Arabia, the UAE and Oman refuse on arrival in 2025-26?
> - Who pays the return ticket of a worker refused on arrival: the airline, the agency or the worker?

<a id="coming-back"></a>

### 3.8 Coming back

<a id="at-the-airport-the-welfare-desk"></a>

#### 3.8.1 At the airport: the welfare desk

The Probashi Kallyan desks at Dhaka, Chattogram and Sylhet, run by the Wage Earners' Welfare Board, meet returning workers free <sup>[76](#o76), [10](#o10)</sup>. They:
- give food and water to workers back from jail or on an outpass <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>;
- help with customs and report lost luggage to Lost and Found <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>;
- take complaints from workers abused or cheated abroad <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>;
- give Tk 5,000 to women back within a year after abuse or fraud <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>;
- may give money to workers back because of war, disaster or lay-off <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>;
- send sick returnees to hospital by ambulance and arrange a stay at the Wage Earners' Centre <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

A deputy director of the Civil Aviation Authority of Bangladesh said in August 2026 that the airport's Probashi Kallyan desk is open 24 hours, and that departing passengers are offered luggage wrapping at low cost <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.

Desks: Dhaka +88 02 8901040, +880 1971 430203, welfaredesk@gmail.com; Chattogram +880 2333300957, +880 1912542185; Sylhet 01321158330, 01973-898375 <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Which desk numbers answer in 2026, given that BMET's charter gives Chattogram 01913631797 and Sylhet 01558277904 <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>?

<a id="deportees-and-outpass-holders"></a>

#### 3.8.2 Deportees and outpass holders

Bangladesh missions issue outpasses (Travel Permits) to send home people without a usable passport; the Abu Dhabi embassy's labour wing, for example, visits jails and the deportation centre to find Bangladeshis and issues them <sup>[160](#o160)</sup>. In Malaysia it costs RM38 from 16 January 2026 <sup>[161](#o161)</sup>. Government agencies count only returnees who come on an outpass, recorded by the Welfare Board's airport desk; according to sources cited by Prothom Alo, 86,621 workers came back empty-handed in 2023, 2,902 of them women <sup>[N28 (2024)](#n28)</sup>; about 89,000 came back this way in 2024 <sup>[N149](#n149)</sup>. Airport sources counted 15,245 travel-pass returns in January to March 2026 and 8,215 in April alone <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>.

- In 2020 at least 255 workers deported from Qatar, Kuwait and Bahrain were jailed at Kashimpur after quarantine under section 54 of the Code of Criminal Procedure (arrest on suspicion), though most had been deported for overstaying or petty offences and pardoned <sup>[N322 (2020)](#n322)</sup>.
- Nearly 70,000 Bangladeshi workers were deported from Saudi Arabia in 2022, mostly for lacking a valid iqama (residence permit) <sup>[N346 (2024)](#n346)</sup>.
- Hasan Gazi of Shyamnagar, Satkhira, went to Kuwait through India in 2018 on Tk 5.5 lakh borrowed from relatives, did various jobs for four years, was detained by police and sent home, and in 2025 was driving an auto-rickshaw in Keraniganj and living in the garage <sup>[N149](#n149)</sup>.
- More than 250 were deported from the US from early 2024 to December 2025; one said he was shackled for 75 hours before landing in Dhaka <sup>[N347](#n347)</sup>. BRAC says at least seven of one flight's 31 had gone to Brazil with BMET clearance and then entered the US irregularly via Mexico, and that such journeys cost Tk 30 to 35 lakh <sup>[N347](#n347)</sup>.
- About 5,000 have been returned from Europe under a 2017 agreement; on 23 October 2026 a charter brought 35 from Italy, Greece and Cyprus, met by BRAC's Migration Programme with emergency help, counselling and fare home <sup>[N348](#n348)</sup>. Later economic help follows counselling and training under the EU-BRAC Prottasha-2 project <sup>[N348](#n348)</sup>. The EU delegation's programme manager said in August 2026 that EU-funded reintegration work began in 2017 and that the projects run through BRAC and IOM are essentially a pilot <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Are deportees questioned or held by police at Dhaka airport in 2026, and on what legal basis?
> - Does SB keep a record of deportation that affects a later passport or emigration clearance?

<a id="customs-and-luggage"></a>

#### 3.8.3 Customs and luggage

Every arriving passenger fills in the baggage declaration (Schedule 1) or submits it online; with nothing dutiable you use the green channel, where up to 5% of bags are scanned at random <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. If by mistake or another unavoidable reason you could not declare, you can declare within 30 days of arrival on the Schedule 1 form to a customs officer not below Assistant Commissioner, under rule 8(3) of the Baggage Rules 2025 <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. A passenger aged 12 or over may bring 65 kg duty-free plus 35 kg of clothes and personal items <sup>[119](#o119)</sup>. The welfare desk helps with customs and lost luggage <sup>[76](#o76)</sup>. A Civil Aviation Authority deputy director said in August 2026 that its investigations found that in 95% of cases of the cut or damaged luggage seen in social-media videos, the damage happened at the airport where the journey began, not in Bangladesh or in transit <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Where does a returnee complain about a customs officer demanding money: NBR (the National Board of Revenue), the customs house, or the welfare desk?

<a id="after-deportation-bans-on-going-back"></a>

#### 3.8.4 After deportation: bans on going back

- **Kuwait:** deportees are fingerprinted at the airport and banned for life <sup>[N323](#n323)</sup>.
- **Malaysia:** deported undocumented migrants are blacklisted for 1 to 5 years, and serious cases for life <sup>[N324 (2017)](#n324)</sup>; all 140 deported from KLIA in September 2026 were blacklisted <sup>[N349](#n349)</sup>.
- **UAE:** a deportee may return only with permission of the ICP (Federal Authority for Identity, Citizenship, Customs and Port Security) chairman <sup>[162 (2021)](#o162)</sup>.
- **Qatar:** someone deported by court judgment may return only by the Minister's decision <sup>[80 (2015)](#o80)</sup>.
- **Italy:** a re-entry ban of 3 to 5 years, longer in some cases, entered in the Schengen Information System, so it also bars entry to EU and other Schengen states <sup>[163](#o163)</sup>.

<a id="help-after-you-get-home"></a>

#### 3.8.5 Help after you get home

**Where to apply.** The Minister said in August 2026 that a returnee who is jobless can apply to the Ministry or the Wage Earners' Welfare Board, and that the government plans to give financial and logistic support suited to the person's occupation and to monitor it <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>. The government's RAISE project says it has brought 2,53,000 returnees under its reintegration programme and gives referral, training and counselling through 35 district offices, working with seven NGOs including BRAC and IOM (the International Organization for Migration) <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>. The SME (small and medium enterprise) Foundation trains returnees in business planning, financial literacy, trade licences, TIN (tax identification number), BSTI (Bangladesh Standards and Testing Institution) certificates and markets, work its managing director called still at a very early stage <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.

**Loans.** PKB's returnee loans are the rehabilitation, women's rehabilitation, special rehabilitation and self-employment loans; it also lends where a migrant's family runs a business at home, and has lent Tk 702 crore to 24,971 people under them, its deputy managing director said in August 2026 <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>. PKB's acting regional head in Sylhet said in April 2026 that the bank lends to returnees who suffered losses at 8% interest, but that in many cases this is not succeeding <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>. Under an agreement between the NSDA (National Skills Development Authority) and PKB, returnees with a skills certificate can get a collateral-free loan of up to Tk 5 lakh, and RPL (recognition of prior learning, certifying skills learnt on the job) is being extended to workers in Saudi Arabia and Singapore, an NSDA director said in August 2026 <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>. An ILO (International Labour Organization) officer said in August 2026 that 94% of returnees want to start a business but find loans hard to get because of paperwork, and that Karmasangsthan Bank now lends to them across the country at 7% interest on just three documents <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - Is PKB's rehabilitation loan at 9% or 8%, and is the collateral-free limit Tk 3 lakh or Tk 5 lakh with a skills certificate <sup>[118](#o118), [N92](#n92), [N107](#n107)</sup>?
> - Which three documents does Karmasangsthan Bank ask returnees for, and up to what amount <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>?

**What reaches people.** The ILO officer said the government recently drew up a reintegration policy for returnees, that many people don't know PKB lends, and that support is usually project-based and stops when the project ends <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>. BRAC's Migration Programme said many people return empty-handed after finding no work, and many have no savings even after 20 to 25 years abroad <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>. RAISE's project director said many women returnees miss out on support because their families sent them abroad but suspect and undervalue them on return, and that the project is considering a union-level officer to record which woman has returned to which house and when <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>. The general secretary of the Ovibashi Nari Sramik Federation said in May 2026 that women who come home after harassment and abuse get hardly any rehabilitation help and struggle to fit back into family and society, and that those returning at 45 to 50 are in a pitiable state; another speaker, Sheikh Romana, said many women return after abuse with a child, and questioned how many returnees really get the help that exists <sup>[N82](#n82)</sup>.

<a id="where-to-complain"></a>

### 3.9 Where to complain

- **Offloading by immigration:** SB's online complaint form at immi.specialbranch.gov.bd/Your_Complain; SS Immigration +88-01713373071 <sup>[156](#o156)</sup>. At the airport: OC Immigration, Dhaka, +880-2-8914226, 01320005902; SSP (Senior Superintendent of Police) Chattogram airport 01320005108; SSP Sylhet airport 01320005107; Additional DIG (Deputy Inspector General) Immigration 01320005035 <sup>[157](#o157)</sup>.
- **Bribes, anywhere in government:** ACC hotline 106, toll-free; in writing to the Chairman, Anti-Corruption Commission, 1 Segunbagicha, Dhaka, or a divisional director <sup>[150](#o150)</sup>.
- **Rights violations by any state body:** the National Human Rights Commission, online at complaint.nhrc.org.bd, or on its form or plain paper by post, fax, email or in person, with any documents, photos or video; free; it replies within seven days if the case is outside its remit, otherwise it investigates and mediates <sup>[159 (2018)](#o159)</sup>.
- **BMET and agencies:** see BMET clearance and the smart card <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.
- **Medical centres (Malaysia):** secretary@probashi.gov.bd <sup>[149](#o149)</sup>.
- **Government offices generally:** the Cabinet Division's GRS at grs.gov.bd <sup>[10](#o10)</sup>.

In TIB's 2025 survey, 61.3% of households that met corruption did not complain, about half of all households did not know where or how to complain, and only 29.5% had heard of the ACC and 1.4% of the GRS <sup>[N325](#n325)</sup>.

<a id="illegal-routes"></a>

## 4 Illegal routes

<a id="in-one-minute-2"></a>

### 4.1 In one minute

Every irregular route sold in Bangladesh ends in one of a few places: a Libyan cell with a phone held to your ear while your family is asked for Tk 25 lakh, a guarded compound in Cambodia or Myanmar where you scam strangers or are beaten, a Russian trench, a Gulf or Maldives street with no permit, a Thai or Malaysian immigration depot, or an airport counter where a fake visa is found and you are sent home <sup>[N245](#n245), [N350](#n350), [N200](#n200), [N351](#n351), [N352](#n352), [N35](#n35), [164 (2024)](#o164), [N353](#n353), [N354](#n354), [N355](#n355)</sup>. The dalal (broker) usually sells it as something legal: a "work visa", a "European work permit", a "free visa", "computer operator" or "construction" work, or "Italy, with some work in Libya first" <sup>[N356](#n356), [N357 (2021)](#n357), [N358](#n358), [N359](#n359), [N360](#n360)</sup>. Common signs: a tourist or visit visa instead of a work visa, a route through Dubai, money split before and after, and a promise that papers will be fixed on arrival <sup>[N165](#n165), [N361](#n361), [N350](#n350), [N181 (2024)](#n181)</sup>. Many victims left with real emigration clearance from BMET (the Bureau of Manpower, Employment and Training) and still ended up in scam compounds <sup>[N362](#n362), [N363](#n363)</sup>. Taking the boat to Italy is often the last stage of an attempt at legal migration that failed <sup>[N356](#n356), [N361](#n361)</sup>. Shariful Islam Hasan of BRAC said in June 2026 that about a lakh people go abroad legally each month, yet many still try risky routes because the legal process is complicated, expensive and in many cases controlled by broker rings <sup>[N99](#n99)</sup>. Cumilla's additional superintendent of police for the Sadar circle said in April 2026 that people fall into dalals' hands more because they do not know the rules, and that those who go through illegal channels are mostly the ones who face more problems <sup>[N109](#n109)</sup>. Besides the boat from Libya, people have gone from Dubai through Oman, Iran and Turkey to Greece (see [From the Gulf overland through Iran and Turkey to Greece](#from-the-gulf-overland-through-iran-and-turkey-to-greece)), and through South America to the United States' border with Mexico (see [The United States through South America and Mexico](#the-united-states-through-south-america-and-mexico)) <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364), [N338 (2024)](#n338)</sup>. In Bangladesh, selling a fake job, trading visas, forging papers for smuggling and smuggling people are crimes with prison terms, and a trafficking victim cannot be charged for illegal entry or a false travel document that resulted from the trafficking <sup>[26](#o26), [165](#o165)</sup>. Families can complain to the CID (the police's Criminal Investigation Department), local police, BMET and the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare, and BRAC and IOM (the International Organization for Migration, the UN migration agency) help with returns (see [Where victims and families get help](#where-victims-and-families-get-help)) <sup>[N365](#n365), [N350](#n350), [10](#o10), [49](#o49), [N366](#n366), [166](#o166)</sup>.

<a id="libya-to-italy-by-boat"></a>

### 4.2 Libya to Italy by boat

<a id="how-it-is-sold"></a>

#### 4.2.1 How it is sold

The offer is Italy, and the price quoted is usually USD 10,000 (about Tk 12.4 lakh): USD 3,000 before leaving and USD 7,000 after reaching Libya <sup>[N356](#n356), [167](#o167)</sup>. An "all-inclusive" package is about USD 14,000 (about Tk 17.4 lakh) <sup>[N356](#n356), [167](#o167)</sup>. In 2026 the Italian Embassy's migration expert said young Bangladeshis pay traffickers Tk 20 to 30 lakh, and families from Cumilla and Chuadanga paid brokers Tk 18 to 34 lakh <sup>[N367](#n367), [N245](#n245), [N350](#n350)</sup>. A common pitch is half the money before departure and the rest "after reaching Italy" <sup>[N350](#n350)</sup>. Others are told they will travel legally, with temporary work in Libya while they wait for the "next visa"; their passports are taken on arrival <sup>[N356](#n356)</sup>. Some are told they are going to Kuwait or Malaysia, and find out Libya was the destination only when their passports are taken in Dubai or Cairo <sup>[N361](#n361)</sup>. A man left unpaid in Dubai was offered Libya by a facilitator there <sup>[N361](#n361)</sup>. In 2025 two men promised jobs in Greece for Tk 15 lakh each were flown to Libya instead <sup>[N368](#n368)</sup>.

A Prothom Alo editorial of 21 February 2026 said local dalals win trust through social ties and point to relatives' success abroad, that the journey begins with a promise of a valid visa and safe travel and the route is changed midway, and that in many cases people are held in Libya or another country and videos of their torture are sent to the family to extract ransom <sup>[N218](#n218)</sup>. Dr Selim Reza, coordinator of the Centre for Migration Studies at North South University, wrote in Prothom Alo on 9 September 2026 that brokers' networks show flashy YouTube videos of an easy way to Europe and use Facebook, imo (a messaging app) and WhatsApp groups to make the offer, keep contact and pass on travel information, and that a Prothom Alo investigation about four years earlier had found Facebook groups used for sending people to Europe across the Mediterranean <sup>[N73](#n73)</sup>.

**How people were drawn in:**

- **Work in Libya first.** Khurshed Mia (35), a vegetable seller in Bhairab, Kishoreganj, said he first went to Libya on 14 June 2023 for BDT 4 lakh on the understanding that the broker would find him work in Libya until he went on to Italy, and that he got work there as a painter <sup>[N369](#n369)</sup>.
- **A legal visa that failed.** The brother of Abul Bashar Akon (32) of Rajoir, Madaripur, who died with his nephew Titu Howlader (19) in the January 2025 sinking, said a BDT 14 lakh contract had been made a year and a half earlier with a broker from Mahendradi to send the two to Italy on an agricultural visa, and that when that broker could not deliver they were forced to decide on the Libya route and made a new contract through another broker <sup>[N370](#n370)</sup>. He said the broker had told the family the two would reach Italy without any problem, that the family first heard of the nephew's death from the broker, and that the broker could no longer be found; the mother said both had told her by phone that they were fine and the broker would give them a "game" (the boat crossing) very soon <sup>[N370](#n370)</sup>.
- **Told by phone after leaving.** Fatema Akter said her husband Shahin Sikdar (26) left home with the broker on 3 February 2024, phoned some days later to say they would be sent from Libya to Italy illegally by sea, and last spoke to the family on the night of 22 March 2024; she said the broker took BDT 18 lakh from them at various times and that she could not stay in her area because of debt pressure <sup>[N291](#n291)</sup>.
- **A neighbour in Libya.** Azmir Hossain (24) of Mirsarai, Chattogram, said Libya-based Shahadat Hossain kept tempting his mother with a job in Italy paying BDT 50,000 a month, took BDT 1.5 lakh in advance, and had Azmir carry the rest of the money himself; a local union parishad (the lowest tier of local government) member said Shahadat is from the same area <sup>[N298 (2024)](#n298)</sup>.
- **Friends already there.** Hridoy Chowdhury, who returned from Libya, told a roundtable at the Prothom Alo office in Dhaka on 18 August 2026 that friends who had gone to Libya told him there would be work there and that later one could go on to Greece or Italy, and that he went with the help of a broker from his own area, Habiganj in Sylhet, whom he trusted because he knew him; the broker is still in Libya <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.

<a id="the-contract-price-and-the-final-price"></a>

#### 4.2.2 The contract price and the final price

**Prices quoted:**

- The migration analyst Asif Munir told Deutsche Welle (DW, Germany's public international broadcaster) that each person needs BDT 16 to 20 lakh for this journey and that international as well as local rings are involved, as quoted in a Prothom Alo editorial of 6 February 2025 <sup>[N371](#n371)</sup>.
- In February 2025, according to police and local sources in Madaripur, the ringleaders of the broker ring taking people to Italy through Libya, from Rajoir and from Bhanga in Faridpur, take BDT 18 to 25 lakh from each person under a contract for Europe, and several times more must be paid if the person is caught by Libyan police or held by mafia there <sup>[N370](#n370)</sup>.
- A broker of another Madaripur ring, speaking anonymously in February 2025, said local brokers only collect people and money for Bangladeshi brokers living in Libya, taking BDT 12 lakh to send a person to Libya and BDT 4 lakh more for the "game" (the sea crossing), and that most local brokers in Madaripur now work for Miraj Howlader of Mridhakandi, Shikarmangal union, Kalkini, whom he called a big mafia figure in Libya <sup>[N302](#n302)</sup>.
- To pay the broker Monir Sheikh of Rajoir, Kuddus Bepari (see [Recruited while abroad or just back](#recruited-while-abroad-or-just-back)) wrote two bighas of land worth BDT 40 lakh to the broker's wife, Hamida Begum; Monir ran a tailoring shop at Rajoir bazar until about two years earlier, married his three daughters to men living in Libya whom locals say are linked to traffickers and mafias there, has bought land worth at least BDT 5 crore in his wife's name in seven months, and, locals say, makes each contract for BDT 16 lakh <sup>[N302](#n302)</sup>.
- On 18 April 2026 the director of Osmani International Airport, Sylhet, said many of the victims heard at that day's roundtable had spent BDT 10 to 15 lakh, and up to BDT 20 lakh, to go abroad <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>.
- According to an account by Bangladesh's ambassador to Libya, Major General Habib Ullah, quoted by former foreign affairs adviser Md Touhid Hossain in Prothom Alo on 27 April 2026, among 113 irregular migrants sent home from Libya one had paid brokers and mafia BDT 82 lakh in stages and another BDT 73 lakh, and when asked how many had paid a broker more than BDT 20 lakh, over fifty of the 113 raised their hands <sup>[N301](#n301)</sup>.
- On 18 August 2026 Shariful Islam Hasan, associate director of BRAC's Migration Programme and Youth Platform, said people from eight to ten districts, including Madaripur, Shariatpur, Sylhet, Sunamganj, Habiganj, Kishoreganj and Narsingdi, try to reach Europe across the Mediterranean, that many first go to Libya through Egypt or Dubai and are physically tortured there, and that some pay BDT 25 lakh, some BDT 30 lakh, and some as much as BDT 65 to 67 lakh <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.

**Cases.** In each case below, the family paid, or was asked for, more money after the migrant reached Libya <sup>[N372 (2024)](#n372), [N291](#n291), [N369](#n369), [N373](#n373), [N374](#n374), [N296 (2024)](#n296), [N375](#n375), [N376 (2024)](#n376), [N310](#n310), [N377](#n377), [N295](#n295), [N378](#n378), [N379](#n379)</sup>:

- **Spain promised, Libya delivered.** The father of a young man from Jagannathpur, Sunamganj, alleges in a case filed in Sylhet in December 2024 that he made a verbal agreement on 15 August 2018 to send his son to Spain for BDT 15 lakh through a man who is the labour affairs secretary of a union Awami League unit, and that the son was instead sent to Libya, where ring members tortured him and threatened to kill him unless more money was paid <sup>[N372 (2024)](#n372)</sup>.
- **BDT 12 to 20 lakh, then money for release.** In 2022 and 2023, 24 young men from Shariatpur and Madaripur were taken from home to be sent to Italy from Libya (see Held, jailed or missing); the broker ring took BDT 12 lakh to 20 lakh from the 24 families in various ways, some of the men were detained by police in Libya on two occasions and jailed, some were held hostage and tortured, and they were freed from captivity and jail after money was collected from their families <sup>[N291](#n291)</sup>.
- **BDT 4 lakh to Libya, BDT 19 lakh in all.** Khurshed Mia, who went to Libya on 14 June 2023 (see [Libya to Italy by boat](#libya-to-italy-by-boat)), said that while working in Libya he agreed with a broker to be taken by sea to Italy for BDT 9 lakh and paid BDT 6 lakh in two rounds without reaching Italy; he said the whole attempt cost him BDT 19 lakh, plus several lakh for treatment, and that he had to sell the land his house stood on <sup>[N369](#n369)</sup>.
- **BDT 3 lakh agreed, BDT 12 lakh ransom.** According to their families, three men from Noli Joynagar village, Mathbaria, Pirojpur, left for Libya through a broker in July 2023 on a contract of BDT 3 lakh and went first to Dubai, after which contact was lost; some time later they called on imo from Libya to report torture, and the ring demanded BDT 12 lakh ransom per person <sup>[N373](#n373)</sup>. The wife of one said in January 2025 she had sold 15 katha of land to raise the BDT 12 lakh ransom, and the father of another said he had paid BDT 15 lakh since his son left and did not know how much more he would have to pay <sup>[N373](#n373)</sup>.
- **BDT 9.5 lakh agreed, BDT 45 lakh taken.** The family of Dipu Mia of Bhairab, Kishoreganj, contracted with a broker from the same village for BDT 9.5 lakh to reach Italy through Libya, and relatives said in August 2025 that the brokers had so far taken BDT 45 lakh and were demanding BDT 25 lakh more <sup>[N374](#n374)</sup>. According to the family, he reached Libya on 23 September 2023, was detained a few days later and passed to a mafia group, and a month and a half after that they got a call showing a torture video and demanding BDT 11 lakh within one day <sup>[N374](#n374)</sup>.
- **BDT 22 to 24 lakh, then BDT 10 lakh for release.** Prothom Alo reported in July 2024 that the Bhairab broker Rasel Mia took BDT 24 lakh from the father of Faruk Mia of Bhairab and BDT 22 lakh each from Tofayel Ahmed and Sabuj Mia for Italy; the father of Shafiqul Islam of Shibpur, Narsingdi, said Rasel took BDT 23 lakh and that BDT 10 lakh was now being demanded to free his son <sup>[N296 (2024)](#n296)</sup>.
- **BDT 15 lakh, then BDT 30 lakh more.** According to his family, Md Rasel Mia (25) of Dharmandal village, Nasirnagar, Brahmanbaria (not the Bhairab broker of the same name), went to Libya in early 2024 through a broker from his own village for BDT 15 lakh, raised by selling the ancestral homestead and borrowing from relatives; he was sold to a local ring on arrival, which sent videos of his torture to Bangladesh and took a further BDT 30 lakh from the family in rounds <sup>[N375](#n375)</sup>. According to the family, the ring demanded BDT 10 lakh more a few days before his death, and when the family could not pay he was tortured and died in Libya on 21 February 2025; the family learned of the death the next day through an expatriate, and his father, who said he had paid BDT 50 lakh in stages by selling his homestead and farmland, alleged his son was killed with a poisonous injection <sup>[N375](#n375)</sup>.
- **BDT 4 lakh, then 4.5, then 2.5 more.** Prothom Alo reported on 21 September 2024 that a broker ring sent 26 young men from 25 families in Goalnagar union, Nasirnagar, Brahmanbaria, and Ratani village, Austagram, Kishoreganj, to Libya on the promise of Italy, each paying BDT 11 lakh; nine came home after torture and harassment, some of the rest were captive and several were missing <sup>[N376 (2024)](#n376)</sup>. Returnees said each of them gave the broker Farhad Mia BDT 4 lakh before leaving, that he took BDT 4.5 lakh more from each while holding them in a camp in Libya, and that after they were caught on the boat and jailed and he got them out, he again took BDT 2.5 lakh from each family promising to send them to Italy through the "game" <sup>[N376 (2024)](#n376)</sup>.
- **BDT 30 lakh demanded, then BDT 42 lakh.** Md Saiful (39) of Sundarganj, Gaibandha, went to Libya through a Bangladeshi broker about two years before November 2026; the ransom calls to his family from August 2025 are set out under no proof of life (see [Ransom and resale](#ransom-and-resale)) <sup>[N310](#n310)</sup>.
- **The "body contract".** According to relatives and neighbours, the family of Saidul Bepari of Madaripur paid BDT 20 lakh ransom to free him in Libya and then agreed a BDT 16 lakh "body contract" through a Bangladeshi broker, an arrangement under which brokers promise to deliver the migrant alive to Italy however long it takes and bear lodging, food and airfare; he died in a Libyan detention camp on 14 February 2025 after torture <sup>[N377](#n377)</sup>.
- **BDT 22 lakh, then BDT 20 lakh more.** According to his family, a BDT 22 lakh contract was made to send Imran Khan of Madaripur to Italy, brokers took BDT 20 lakh more while he was held in a detention camp in Benghazi, and he was shot dead by pirates on a migrant boat on 1 November 2025, which the family learned from a broker on 18 November <sup>[N295](#n295)</sup>.
- **BDT 25 lakh, then BDT 22.5 lakh more.** According to relatives and local sources, a broker first took BDT 25 lakh from Tachhir Fakir of Rajoir, Madaripur, for the journey to Italy; brokers in Libya then held him in a detention camp and took BDT 20 lakh ransom from his family and another BDT 2.5 lakh said to be for treatment, and he died in a Libyan hospital in May 2026 <sup>[N378](#n378)</sup>. The family sold all its land to pay <sup>[N378](#n378)</sup>.
- **BDT 60 lakh in three rounds.** Mizanur Rahman of Phulsuti village, Nagarkanda, Faridpur, alleged at a human chain outside the Faridpur Deputy Commissioner's office on 14 October 2026 that a Faridpur trafficking ring, having promised to take him to Italy, held and tortured him in Libya and took BDT 60 lakh in three rounds <sup>[N379](#n379)</sup>.

<a id="the-path"></a>

#### 4.2.3 The path

1. <a id="the-path-item-1"></a>**Dhaka to Dubai** on a tourist visa <sup>[N361](#n361)</sup>. Azmir Hossain said he reached Dubai on 21 March 2024, where the broker's men met him at the airport and kept 30 to 35 people together in a room of a small house for two days <sup>[N298 (2024)](#n298)</sup>.
2. <a id="the-path-item-2"></a>**Dubai to Benghazi**, directly or through Egypt; when checks at Dhaka airport tighten, groups are sent through India and Sri Lanka instead <sup>[N356](#n356), [N361](#n361)</sup>. One 2026 victim was routed Chennai, Sri Lanka, Dubai, Alexandria, Benghazi and Tripoli; another group went through Mumbai, Saudi Arabia and Egypt <sup>[N380](#n380), [N350](#n350)</sup>. According to Palong Model police station and relatives, 24 Shariatpur and Madaripur men taken from home in 2022 and 2023 were sent first to the United Arab Emirates, then through Egypt to Libya <sup>[N291](#n291)</sup>. Azmir Hossain said his group was flown from Dubai to Egypt, held 24 hours in a warehouse-like place in the airport area, and put on another plane to Libya <sup>[N298 (2024)](#n298)</sup>.
3. <a id="the-path-item-3"></a>**Across Libya by road**, west through Ajdabiya, Sirte and Shwerayif to Zuwara and Zawiya, the main departure points since 2023 <sup>[N356](#n356)</sup>. Entry to Benghazi is on clearances from the Libyan National Army's Military Investment Authority, the armed force that controls the east <sup>[N356](#n356)</sup>.
4. <a id="the-path-item-4"></a>**The boat to Lampedusa**, Italy's southern island, usually 24 to 36 hours in fair weather <sup>[N356](#n356)</sup>. The crossing itself is known as the "game"; small boats are mainly used and are loaded beyond capacity <sup>[N370](#n370)</sup>.

Md Touhid Hossain, the former foreign affairs adviser, wrote in April 2026 that the main route to Italy by sea used to be a flight to Kenya, then overland to Libya, then the boat, that a person boards the plane to Kenya on a false story of a safari or tourism, and that along the way people change hands several times like goods <sup>[N301](#n301)</sup>. He also wrote that because surveillance and restrictions on the route to Italy had recently increased, traffickers were now using the Greece route <sup>[N301](#n301)</sup>. UNHCR (the UN refugee agency) data show that in 2026 Bangladeshis were for the first time the top nationality among sea crossings to Greece, Prothom Alo reported on 30 July 2026 <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>.

The chain is run in pieces: brokers in Bangladesh recruit and collect money, facilitators in the UAE, Egypt, Kuwait, Turkey, India and Sri Lanka move groups, and Libyan airport sponsors, armed groups, drivers and coastal smugglers control everything inside Libya <sup>[N361](#n361)</sup>. The Counter Terrorism and Transnational Crime (CTTC) unit of the police identified a Libya-based trafficker who had appointed 40 brokers across Bangladesh whose job was to find young people wanting to go abroad, according to a Prothom Alo editorial of 25 February 2024 <sup>[N339 (2024)](#n339)</sup>.

<a id="ransom-and-resale"></a>

#### 4.2.4 Ransom and resale

- **Ransom.** Smugglers call Bangladeshis "gold" because a family at home will answer ransom calls <sup>[N361](#n361)</sup>. Men are held, tortured and forced to phone home for more money; five Chuadanga men were held 10 months in a room with 57 others <sup>[N350](#n350)</sup>. In July 2026 families of six men from Manoharganj, Cumilla, were asked Tk 25 lakh per person, and in November 2026 the same sum was reportedly being demanded for each of more than 300 men from Bhairab, Kishoreganj <sup>[N245](#n245), [N350](#n350)</sup>. People in the migration sector told Prothom Alo in July 2026 that workers bound for Italy or Greece are sent to Libya, tortured in many camps there, and held hostage until BDT 25 to 30 lakh is extracted <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>. Bangladeshi and Libyan smugglers stage kidnappings together <sup>[N356](#n356)</sup>. Md Touhid Hossain wrote in April 2026 that many Bengalis are among those who torture the hostages in Libya, and that the people who collect ransom from families in Bangladesh by showing torture videos live undisturbed in the same society <sup>[N301](#n301)</sup>.
- **No proof of life.** According to investigators, on 5 August 2025 the wife of Md Saiful of Sundarganj, Gaibandha, got a call from a Libyan mobile number saying he was in the callers' custody, that BDT 30 lakh ransom must be paid or he would be killed, and that no one in the family would be allowed to speak to him until half the ransom was paid <sup>[N310](#n310)</sup>. The CID said the family sent BDT 5 lakh in all between 10 and 25 August 2025 from a bKash (mobile money) shop at Bamandanga Hall Mor, Sundarganj, to several mobile numbers the accused gave, and that even after the money was received the family was not put in touch with Saiful <sup>[N310](#n310)</sup>. The father was then contacted from another number and asked for a further BDT 42 lakh in the name of rescuing his son, and was told his son was held by two men in Libya named Mokarram and Anwar from whom the caller had got the father's number; the father said he would pay nothing more unless he could speak to his son directly, and no more calls came to him or to his son's wife <sup>[N310](#n310)</sup>.
- **No one guarantees release.** Families of two hostages in the Bhairab case said in July 2024 that large ransoms were being demanded but that they could find no one who could assure them that paying would get the men released <sup>[N296 (2024)](#n296)</sup>. According to local people in Bhairab, when the hostages phoned home begging to be freed, the broker Rasel Mia and his ring members hid and cut off contact with the families; the families then went to community leaders for a remedy and got no result <sup>[N296 (2024)](#n296)</sup>.
- **Families stay silent.** The wife of one Mathbaria hostage refused to name the brokers to Prothom Alo in January 2025, saying she wanted her husband back, and the family of another refused to speak at all, believing someone known to the brokers lived near their home and that the torture would increase if word got out <sup>[N373](#n373)</sup>.
- **Sold between gangs.** Seven Bangladeshis were sold for USD 17,000 together, and the buyer demanded USD 12,000 each <sup>[N361](#n361)</sup>. Azmir Hossain said everyone's passport was taken as soon as they reached Libya in March 2024, that he was beaten whenever he mentioned the promised job, and that BDT 6 lakh was collected from his family in stages by phone <sup>[N298 (2024)](#n298)</sup>. He said the broker then sold him to another group at a high price and left him in a Libyan desert to be handed over; he escaped with a Libyan citizen's help and took shelter with a Bangladeshi he knew, and the broker then came and took him back <sup>[N298 (2024)](#n298)</sup>. Suman Mia of Nasirnagar said in September 2024 that he paid the broker BDT 11 lakh in all and was told he would be taken to Italy within a week; 60 people were put on a boat from Benghazi, and after it had run for an hour they understood they had all been sold <sup>[N376 (2024)](#n376)</sup>. Enamul Haque of Lalurtuk village, Nasirnagar, said he spent BDT 22 lakh to send two sons (land sold for BDT 7 lakh, a BDT 12 lakh loan and savings), that one son was "sold four times", and that he last spoke to the other a year earlier; both were missing in September 2024 <sup>[N376 (2024)](#n376)</sup>.
- **Extra charges.** Charges added in Libya push the total to USD 15,000 to 17,000 (about Tk 18.7 to 21.2 lakh) <sup>[N356](#n356), [167](#o167)</sup>. In the last holding houses before the sea ("game houses") smugglers demand more even from people told the trip was fully paid <sup>[N356](#n356)</sup>. Those told they can pay after reaching Europe are made to work in Libya to pay off the debt <sup>[N356](#n356)</sup>.
- **Paying does not free you.** In a 2025 case the families paid Tk 21.8 lakh and Tk 16 lakh in ransom, and the men were then handed to Libyan police and held 45 days <sup>[N368](#n368)</sup>. People caught at sea by the Libyan Coast Guard report being handed to Bangladeshi networks on the coast who demand more <sup>[N356](#n356)</sup>.
- **Killed when the family could not pay.** At the Faridpur human chain on 14 October 2026, Taslima Begum said her husband Habib was killed because the family could not pay a ransom of BDT 10 lakh; she now lives with their three children at her father's house <sup>[N379](#n379)</sup>.

<a id="held-jailed-or-missing"></a>

#### 4.2.5 Held, jailed or missing

- **In the camps.** Suman Mia said the 60 sold off the Benghazi boat were held in a camp for a month and a half, beaten every day and fed once in 24 hours, given two litres of salt water for bathing and the toilet and one bread at night, and made to do cleaning work from morning to evening at the house of a man named Ahammad, whose son beat them with a pistol when they did not work <sup>[N376 (2024)](#n376)</sup>. Azmir Hossain said he was locked in a room and beaten regularly for trying to escape and went eight days without food; he reached home on 5 May 2024 in a critical condition, 41 days after leaving home according to Prothom Alo, and his mother said he is now mentally ill from blows to the head and that she owes BDT 6 lakh <sup>[N298 (2024)](#n298)</sup>. Returnees told the ambassador to Libya before their flight home in spring 2026 that one had eaten rice once after twenty days, five men sharing one bowl with no curry, that another got a handful after twenty-five days, and that a piece of bread was thrown at them "the way food is given to a dog" and they were beaten as well <sup>[N301](#n301)</sup>.
- **Raids and jail.** Khurshed Mia said law-enforcement officers raided the fourth-floor flat where 11 of them lived at about 1:30 am on 10 July 2024 and he hid for two hours on the window ledge; later he was kept in a place with 210 other people, where a raid at 10 pm took away 160 while he hid for four hours in a tree <sup>[N369](#n369)</sup>. Returnees from Nasirnagar and Austagram said that two and a half to three months after the last payment the broker sold them, that after three months in jail they reached their families through a Pakistani man with the help of a man from Bhairab, and could come home only after each paid a further BDT 1 lakh 20 thousand for the air ticket and other costs; they returned between 7 August and 14 September 2024 after release from Libyan jail <sup>[N376 (2024)](#n376)</sup>. Hridoy Chowdhury said that after reaching Libya he worked for a company for two months in a row, was then taken to Benghazi and boarded a boat for Greece, and that after about 14 hours on the boat the coast guard detained them and sent them back to Libya, where he was held as a prisoner in a jail in Tobruk <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>. He said he finally got out of the jail by contacting his broker, and that he had to come home empty-handed despite spending a huge amount of money <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.
- **Escape.** Mizanur Rahman said that, still not released after the payments and seeing how severe the torture was, he broke a window, escaped and gave himself up to Libyan police, and later came home in 2026 through IOM; eleven people tortured by the same ring came home from Libya through IOM in 2026 and joined the human chain in Faridpur <sup>[N379](#n379)</sup>.
- **Missing.** Prothom Alo reported on 13 July 2024 that at least 24 people from Bhairab and elsewhere in Kishoreganj, Habiganj and Narsingdi went to Libya at about the same time through a broker ring led by Rasel Mia of Bhairab to cross to Italy by sea, that none had reached Italy, and that some were hostages of kidnappers and others were in Libyan jails <sup>[N296 (2024)](#n296)</sup>. According to relatives and police, 24 young men from Shariatpur and Madaripur who were taken from home in 2022 and 2023 went missing in Libya; relatives claim none of the 24 has contacted family since 21 and 22 March 2024 <sup>[N291](#n291)</sup>. Riaz Akon, one of their relatives, said his family gave the broker BDT 20 lakh, raised by selling land and a cattle farm, to send his younger brother to Italy, had had no trace of him for a year, and that the broker gave no information when asked <sup>[N291](#n291)</sup>.

<a id="work-in-libya"></a>

#### 4.2.6 Work in Libya

Khurshed Mia said that in Libya, after a month without work, he got a job at a shopping mall "in exchange for 500 dinars" that was to pay 1,400 dinars at the end of the month but paid him half, and then worked four months at an oil company for 1,200 dinars a month <sup>[N369](#n369)</sup>. He said a mafia group attacked the building the broker had moved him to and he jumped from the first floor to save himself, injuring his arm and leg; he got no treatment for five days, the broker's men moved him from a private hospital to a government hospital because they could not bear the cost, he had five operations in three and a half months there, and he came home on 13 February 2025 unable to walk <sup>[N369](#n369)</sup>.

<a id="deaths"></a>

#### 4.2.7 Deaths

- **May 2020, Mizda.** According to Libya's then government, a trafficker's family killed 26 Bangladeshis and four Africans in Mizda, Libya <sup>[N381 (2020)](#n381)</sup>.
- **February 2024, off Tunisia.** On 15 February 2024 a boat carrying 52 migrants caught fire and sank in the Mediterranean off the Tunisian coast; 8 Bangladeshis died, 26 others were rescued and one was seriously injured, according to a Prothom Alo editorial of 25 February 2024 <sup>[N339 (2024)](#n339)</sup>. According to relatives, police and local sources, several young men from Rajoir, Madaripur, and Muksudpur, Gopalganj, left home for Italy on 14 January 2024, flew through Dubai to Libya, and on 14 February left Libya on an engine boat through brokers; eight of them died on the boat off Tunisia (five from Rajoir and three from Muksudpur), along with one Pakistani <sup>[N145 (2024)](#n145)</sup>. Relatives of the eight told journalists at Dhaka airport on 2 May 2024 that brokers had put 52 people on a small boat that could take 30, that the eight who died had been forced to stay below the deck, and that when they tried to come out for lack of oxygen the brokers beat them and sent them back down; the relatives said they died of the torture and lack of oxygen <sup>[N145 (2024)](#n145)</sup>. The brother of one of the dead, Sajal Bairagi (22) of Sendia village, Rajoir, said the family gave BDT 14 lakh to a broker named Mosharraf by selling land and cows and borrowing <sup>[N145 (2024)](#n145)</sup>.
- **January 2025, off Libya.** According to the Bangladesh embassy in Libya, a boat carrying 56 people trying to reach Italy from Libya sank in the Mediterranean on 25 January 2025 and 23 bodies washed up on the beach between 28 and 31 January; the bodies were decomposed and not formally identified, but the embassy, citing local sources, said nearly all were Bangladeshi <sup>[N370](#n370)</sup>. According to relatives, police and local sources, two Rajoir men left home on 1 January 2025, flew from Dhaka through Dubai to Libya, were sent by the broker to the coast, and left the Libyan coast on an engine boat on 24 January <sup>[N370](#n370)</sup>. Intelligence sources said in February 2025 that, besides the two dead, eight more people from Rajoir upazila, all aged 20 to 30, were reported missing, though it was not confirmed whether they were on the boat that sank on 25 January <sup>[N370](#n370)</sup>. Relatives said at least 12 people from Madaripur and Faridpur were among those killed, according to a Prothom Alo editorial of 6 February 2025 <sup>[N371](#n371)</sup>. The same editorial reported that the father of Hridoy Howlader of Kamarkhali, Faridpur, said the group was to be taken to Italy by ship on the night of 24 January, that many refused to board when a small boat was offered instead, and that those who refused were shot dead while those who boarded drowned <sup>[N371](#n371)</sup>. It also reported that Sujan Faraji of Madaripur, who died in the sinking, set out with BDT 16 lakh taken from his aunt without telling his father, who had not agreed to the journey, and that a broker named Monir Sheikh of Majumdarkandi, Rajoir, had contracted to take him to Italy for BDT 15 lakh <sup>[N371](#n371)</sup>. Abul Bashar Akon and his nephew Titu Howlader also died (see [Libya to Italy by boat](#libya-to-italy-by-boat)) <sup>[N370](#n370)</sup>. Kuddus Bepari, sent from Dhaka to Egypt in early November 2024 and on to Libya, and his brother-in-law Sujon Howlader (39), who also paid the broker Monir BDT 16 lakh, were put on an engine boat from the Benghazi coast on 24 January 2025 after about two months in a detention house; the boat sank within hours, Sujon's death was confirmed by his wife Munni Begum, and Kuddus was missing, believed dead by his family <sup>[N302](#n302)</sup>. Madaripur's Additional Superintendent of Police (Administration and Finance), Bhaskar Saha, said in February 2025 that by family accounts three people from Rajoir upazila had died and seven were missing, presumed dead, after the recent sinking, that no family had yet come to file a case, and that police were hunting several named brokers <sup>[N302](#n302)</sup>.
- **November 2025, off Al Khums.** A boat with 26 Bangladeshis capsized off Al Khums and four died <sup>[N382](#n382)</sup>.
- **March 2026, Libya to Greece.** At least 18 Bangladeshis died on a boat that left Tobruk, Libya, for Greece, carrying 38 Bangladeshis among 43 people <sup>[N383](#n383)</sup>. Twelve of the dead were from Sunamganj <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>. Md Touhid Hossain wrote in Prothom Alo on 27 April 2026 that a boat that left Libya for Greece on 21 March 2026 with 48 passengers drifted for six days before 26 people were rescued alive, 21 of them Bangladeshis, and that 20 of the 22 dead or missing were reported to be Bangladeshi <sup>[N301](#n301)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 1 April 2026 said the family of Shayek Ahmed (23) of Sunamganj gave brokers BDT 12 lakh by selling their cows and land, that the rubber boat he left on from Libya for Greece on 21 March lost its way and drifted for six days, that he died of starvation when the food ran out, and that 12 young men from Sunamganj died on that journey and their bodies were thrown into the sea <sup>[N313](#n313)</sup>. According to a survivor, the boat carrying 38 people from Libya lost its way and drifted six days; 18 died for lack of food and clean water, their bodies were kept on the boat two days and then thrown into the sea, and the survivors were taken to a camp after the boat reached the Greek coast on 27 March 2026 <sup>[N384](#n384)</sup>.
- **April 2026, from Tajoura.** On 4 April 2026 a wooden boat that left Tajoura, Libya, for the Italian island of Lampedusa with about 105 passengers sank; 32 people were rescued alive and 2 bodies recovered, the other 71 were missing, and Bangladeshis were among both the rescued and the missing, Md Touhid Hossain wrote <sup>[N301](#n301)</sup>.
- **At sea.** The State Minister for Expatriates' Welfare alleged that smugglers abandon weak or sick passengers at sea and often throw bodies into the water <sup>[N383](#n383)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many people, and how many Bangladeshis, were on the boat that left Libya for Greece in March 2026, and how many Bangladeshis died: at least 18 of 38, or 20 of 22 dead or missing out of 48 aboard?
> - How many from Rajoir died or went missing in the January 2025 sinking: two dead and eight more missing (intelligence sources), or three dead and seven missing (police, from family accounts) <sup>[N370](#n370), [N302](#n302)</sup>?

<a id="arrival-in-italy"></a>

#### 4.2.8 Arrival in Italy

People landing are taken to a hotspot (a first-reception centre) for checks, photos and fingerprints <sup>[168](#o168)</sup>. Italy lists Bangladesh as a safe country of origin and rejected 88.66% of Bangladeshi asylum claims at first decision in 2025 <sup>[169](#o169), [N385](#n385)</sup>. Italy returned over 120 Bangladeshis in 2025 up to early November, including four on 31 October, two of whom had arrived from Libya without a visa a month before <sup>[170](#o170)</sup>.

<a id="coming-home"></a>

#### 4.2.9 Coming home

Men who went this way and failed have come home with debts of more than Tk 10 lakh <sup>[N361](#n361)</sup>. Five Chuadanga families lost about Tk 1.47 crore in fees and ransom <sup>[N350](#n350)</sup>. Mashiur Rahman of Madaripur, who came home on 1 September 2026 with a group of Bangladeshis returned from detention in Libya with IOM's help, said he had left his own restaurant business to try for Italy and came back having lost BDT 32 lakh <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>.

<a id="the-numbers-1"></a>

#### 4.2.10 The numbers

- **Arrivals in Italy by sea:** 20,164 Bangladeshis in 2025 and 6,160 from 1 January to 6 November 2026, more than any other nationality both years <sup>[171](#o171), [172](#o172)</sup>. BRAC, citing Frontex (the EU border agency), said 20,259 Bangladeshis entered Europe across the Mediterranean in 2025, and that in the first six months of 2026, 4,249 Bangladeshis entered Italy this way and 3,608 went from Libya to Greece by sea <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>. Prothom Alo reported on 5 September 2026 that 66,316 people of all nationalities crossed the Mediterranean to Italy in 2025 and 1,126 of them died or went missing at sea, and that of 14,194 who crossed up to June 2026 the largest share, 32% or 3,691 people, were Bangladeshis <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>. According to CID information cited in a Prothom Alo editorial of 1 April 2026, 17,000 Bangladeshis were sent to Italy by illegal routes in the previous year <sup>[N313](#n313)</sup>. According to UNHCR, as cited in a Prothom Alo editorial of 25 February 2024, 13 per cent of the 5,236 refugees and migrant workers who went to Italy by sea were Bangladeshi citizens; the editorial does not state the period <sup>[N339 (2024)](#n339)</sup>.
- **Share of the route:** Bangladeshis were 50% of people leaving Libya in December 2024 and 39% in December 2025; 88% of all arrivals in Italy in 2025 left from Libya <sup>[N386](#n386), [N387](#n387)</sup>. Frontex counted 2,510 Bangladeshis among 8,489 crossings on the Central Mediterranean route in January to April 2026 <sup>[N361](#n361)</sup>. Bangladeshis were again in 2026 the top nationality among people crossing the Mediterranean to Italy, according to UNHCR data reported by Prothom Alo on 30 July 2026 <sup>[N24](#n24)</sup>.
- **Over the years:** 4,141 Bangladeshis arrived by this route in 2020, 7,838 in 2021 and 15,228 in 2022; Frontex counts about 110,000 from 2009 to June 2026 <sup>[N361](#n361), [N350](#n350)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 6 February 2025 stated, giving no source, that 84,000 Bangladeshis went to Europe by sea from 2009 to 2024 and that on average at least 100 Bangladeshis die each year when boats sink <sup>[N371](#n371)</sup>. Shariful Islam Hasan of BRAC said on 18 April 2026 that Bangladeshis are the largest group in the world crossing the Mediterranean to Europe, a situation that has lasted a decade <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>.
- **All irregular routes to Europe:** IOM counted 24,318 Bangladeshis reaching Europe by irregular sea and land routes in 2025, up 59% on 2024 <sup>[N388](#n388)</sup>. The general secretary of the Sylhet District Press Club said on 18 April 2026, giving no source, that over the previous 10 years at least 10,000 people had died or gone missing, more than two and a half lakh had been trafficked abroad by illegal routes and at least 15,000 had been jailed in various countries <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>. Shariful Islam Hasan of BRAC said at a roundtable in Cox's Bazar on 6 June 2026 that about one lakh Bangladeshis have been detained while trying to cross the Mediterranean in the past 16 years, and that about six and a half lakh Bangladeshis currently have asylum applications lodged in various countries <sup>[N99](#n99)</sup>.
- **Clearance:** Prothom Alo reported on 5 September 2026 that only 100 people took BMET clearance to go to Libya in 2025 and 63 in 2026, which it reads as meaning the workers reach Libya through the Middle East and other countries <sup>[N18](#n18)</sup>.
- **Where people come from:** Shariful Islam Hasan of BRAC said on 18 April 2026 that people from 8 to 10 districts, among them Madaripur, Shariatpur, Sylhet, Sunamganj and Habiganj, are losing their lives going this way <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>. By the ambassador to Libya's account, about two-thirds of 113 returnees on one spring 2026 flight identified themselves as from Madaripur <sup>[N301](#n301)</sup>.
- **Deaths:** 1,718 people died or went missing on the Central Mediterranean in 2024, one for every 39 arrivals, and about 765 by 7 April 2026, over 460 more than a year earlier <sup>[N386](#n386), [N389](#n389)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 21 February 2026 says, citing UNHCR data, that Bangladeshis were the largest group among migrants who crossed the Mediterranean to Italy from January to October 2024, as in 2023, and states, without naming a source, that more than 25,000 people drowned in the Mediterranean in the past decade, of whom at least 500 each year were Bangladeshi <sup>[N218](#n218)</sup>.
- **Returns:** at least 10,000 Bangladeshis have been flown home from Libya with IOM's help since 2017, including 175 on 26 February 2026 <sup>[N361](#n361), [N366](#n366)</sup>. In 2023 the Embassy in Tripoli could not get Libyan exit permits to send home about 2,000 people <sup>[N356](#n356)</sup>. According to Bangladesh embassy sources in Tripoli cited in a Prothom Alo editorial of 6 February 2025, 4,200 Bangladeshis had been freed from various detention centres in Libya and sent home in the previous year and a half, and more than a thousand irregular Bangladeshis were still held in Libya <sup>[N371](#n371)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 25 February 2024 stated that BRAC's Migration Programme rescued and brought home 629 people in 2022 who had been living abroad without valid papers, and that they had been taken to Libya with the promise of attractive jobs in Europe <sup>[N339 (2024)](#n339)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - How many Bangladeshis reached Italy by sea in 2025: 20,164, the 20,259 BRAC gives from Frontex for all of Europe, or the 17,000 sent to Italy by illegal routes "in the past year", from CID information cited in a Prothom Alo editorial of 1 April 2026?
> - How many Bangladeshis reached Italy by sea in January to June 2026: 4,249 (BRAC, from Frontex) or 3,691 (Prothom Alo, 5 September 2026)?
> - How many Bangladeshis die on the Mediterranean in a typical year: the "at least 100" or the "at least 500" two Prothom Alo editorials give, and from what source?

<a id="the-law"></a>

#### 4.2.11 The law

- **Bangladesh:** under the 2026 Act, organising this journey is migrant smuggling, holding people for ransom is a form of exploitation under the trafficking offence, and the Act also covers offences committed abroad against Bangladeshis (see [The law in Bangladesh](#the-law-in-bangladesh)) <sup>[165](#o165)</sup>. The Minister for Expatriates' Welfare said on 18 April 2026 that the government had declared zero tolerance of the "mafia syndicate" at home and abroad and was determined to identify the Bangladeshi members of trafficking rings in Libya and elsewhere, bring them back and try them <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>. A Prothom Alo editorial of 6 February 2025 stated that some licensed manpower-exporting firms are behind these broker rings and send people abroad through them, and that cases against many such firms in the past led to no action because many of those involved were influential people linked to the then ruling party <sup>[N371](#n371)</sup>.
- **Libya:** in April 2026 a Libyan court jailed four traffickers for up to 22 years <sup>[N390](#n390)</sup>.
- **Italy:** entering or staying without permission is an offence fined EUR 5,000 to 10,000 (about Tk 6.98 to 13.96 lakh) <sup>[173](#o173), [167](#o167)</sup>. If removal can't happen at once, the Questore (head of the provincial immigration police) can hold the person in a CPR, a detention centre before removal, for three months <sup>[174](#o174)</sup>. Expulsion brings a 3 to 5-year ban entered in the Schengen Information System, which bars every EU and Schengen country <sup>[163](#o163)</sup>. An Italian court did recognise one Bangladeshi kidnapped and tortured in Libya until his family paid as a trafficking victim <sup>[N391](#n391)</sup>.

**Cases against brokers in Bangladesh:**

- **May 2024, Madaripur.** Sajal Bairagi's brother said the broker was telling the family not to file a case and had said he would return some money but was not giving it, and that, being poor and unable to afford a case, the family was trying to settle with the broker <sup>[N145 (2024)](#n145)</sup>. The Madaripur Superintendent of Police, Masud Alam, said police would give all legal help if relatives of the eight dead filed a case, but that the victims had little interest in filing cases <sup>[N145 (2024)](#n145)</sup>.
- **June 2024, Chattogram.** After returning, Azmir Hossain filed a case in a Chattogram court against six people, including the Libya-based broker and his brother; his lawyer said it was a criminal case over cheating and torture in Libya and that the court took cognisance of the complaint and ordered an investigation <sup>[N298 (2024)](#n298)</sup>.
- **July 2024, Bhairab.** On 5 July 2024, when the broker Rasel Mia came to his home in Jagannathpur, Bhairab, victims' families surrounded and beat him and handed him to police, who sent him to Kishoreganj district jail under the trafficking Act; the officer-in-charge of Bhairab police station said the father of one victim filed a case after the arrest and Rasel was sent to jail in that case <sup>[N296 (2024)](#n296)</sup>. Rasel Mia admitted under police questioning that he took money on the promise of sending people to Italy, and said he had made all the arrangements but could not send everyone because new problems had arisen on the sea route, at which everyone grew impatient <sup>[N296 (2024)](#n296)</sup>.
- **September 2024, Nasirnagar.** The Nasirnagar Upazila Nirbahi Officer (the government's chief administrator of the sub-district) said the administration had not known of the 26 men's cases until journalists told them, that none of the victims had complained to it, and that legal action would follow an investigation if a complaint came; relatives had held a human chain demanding the brokers' trial and compensation <sup>[N376 (2024)](#n376)</sup>. One man the families accuse, a cousin of the alleged ring leader, told Prothom Alo he had himself gone to Libya through that cousin, spent a month and a half in jail there with the others and 18 months in Libya in all, and took money from no one <sup>[N376 (2024)](#n376)</sup>.
- **2024 to January 2025, Mathbaria.** The wife of one Mathbaria hostage filed a case under the trafficking Act at Mathbaria police station in 2024; the station's officer-in-charge said in January 2025 that the investigation had been given to the district detective branch and that it could say what progress there was <sup>[N373](#n373)</sup>.
- **February 2025, Rajoir.** After the January 2025 sinking, local brokers in Rajoir went into hiding for fear of arrest; Madaripur's Additional Superintendent of Police said police were investigating the broker ring, and the Additional Deputy Commissioner said the district administration had started work through the embassy to bring the bodies home <sup>[N370](#n370)</sup>.
- **February 2025, Shariatpur.** Relatives of the 24 missing men filed 11 cases against Rashed Khan and his associates in the Shariatpur court and at Palong Model police station; the CID arrested him in Jatrabari, Dhaka, on 14 February 2025 in a case under the trafficking Act filed by the father of a missing man from Kalkini, Madaripur, and a Shariatpur magistrate sent him to jail the next day, fixing a later date to hear the police's remand request and his bail application <sup>[N291](#n291)</sup>. On 15 February, on hearing of the arrest, relatives of the missing men began a hunger strike in the grounds of the Shariatpur District and Sessions Judge's court demanding exemplary punishment and the tracing of the missing men <sup>[N291](#n291)</sup>.
- **February 2025, Nasirnagar.** After Md Rasel Mia's death the broker from his village fled with his family; the officer-in-charge of Nasirnagar police station said legal steps would be taken if the victim's family gave a written complaint <sup>[N375](#n375)</sup>.
- **October 2026, Faridpur.** Asked about the returnees' demand for trial of the Faridpur ring, the Additional Superintendent of Police (Crime and Ops) of Faridpur said on 14 October 2026 that legal action would be taken if the victims' families complained, and that steps would follow investigation if anyone had filed a case; one victim at the human chain asked, "Where do I go to get justice?" <sup>[N379](#n379)</sup>.
- **November 2026, Gaibandha.** The father of the Gaibandha hostage filed a case under the trafficking Act at Sundarganj police station on 5 September 2025, the case was sent to the CID for investigation, and on 12 November 2026 the CID arrested two men from Chandpur village, Singra, at Singra Bazar, Natore; the CID said it was still gathering information on where in Libya the son was held and on rescuing him <sup>[N310](#n310)</sup>.

<a id="where-to-get-help"></a>

#### 4.2.12 Where to get help

- **In Libya:** the Bangladesh Embassy in Tripoli, the Foreign Ministry, the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare and IOM organise the return flights <sup>[N366](#n366)</sup>. Libya's Anti-Illegal Migration Agency rescued 13 kidnapped Bangladeshis from a Tripoli hideout in 2026 <sup>[N392](#n392)</sup>. When 113 migrants were flown home in spring 2026, IOM paid the whole cost from the airfare to their travel back to their own villages, Md Touhid Hossain wrote; they went home with nothing <sup>[N301](#n301)</sup>.
- **In Italy:** Bangladesh Embassy, Rome, Via dell'Antartide 5-7, 00144 Rome, +39 06 808 3595, emergency +39 3511 766 537 <sup>[175](#o175)</sup>. Italy's anti-trafficking helpline 800 290 290 is free, anonymous and open 24 hours <sup>[176](#o176)</sup>.
- **At home:** a family that paid ransom can file a case with local police, as one victim did at Haziganj police station, Chandpur, in December 2025, which led the CID to arrest a man accused of coordinating the ransom demands (see [Where victims and families get help](#where-victims-and-families-get-help)) <sup>[N368](#n368), [N350](#n350)</sup>. The Deputy Commissioner of Pirojpur, asked in January 2025 about three local men held for ransom in Libya, said the administration has little it can do in such cases, but that if the family complains it can inform the Ministry of Foreign Affairs in writing <sup>[N373](#n373)</sup>. A union parishad member in Karerhat, Mirsarai, said he phoned the Libya-based broker several times to get Azmir Hossain sent home and saw the marks of beating on Azmir's head and body on a video call <sup>[N298 (2024)](#n298)</sup>. In its statement on the Gaibandha case on 13 November 2026 the CID asked everyone to verify the legality of the person, the agency, the visa and the employing firm before going abroad <sup>[N310](#n310)</sup>. BRAC has started a campaign with the slogan "No going abroad by sea", working through police, mosque imams, religious leaders, schools and social media, Shariful Islam Hasan said on 18 August 2026 <sup>[N107](#n107)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What are the Bangladesh Embassy in Tripoli's working phone, WhatsApp and email for families of people held in Libya? Its website's contact page is empty.
> - Does IOM Bangladesh take requests from families directly to trace a person held in Libya, and on what number?

<a id="from-the-gulf-overland-through-iran-and-turkey-to-greece"></a>

### 4.3 From the Gulf overland through Iran and Turkey to Greece

<a id="how-it-is-sold-1"></a>

#### 4.3.1 How it is sold

Faridul Alam of Chandanaish, Chattogram, who had worked in Dubai since 2003, told a columnist writing in Prothom Alo in December 2024 that he made a verbal contract of BDT 5 lakh with a Bangladeshi broker from Noakhali to be taken as far as Greece: BDT 50,000 in advance, BDT 1.5 lakh after reaching Iran and the remaining BDT 3 lakh after reaching Turkey <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>. By his account, after he reached Iran a relative in Dubai deposited BDT 1.5 lakh into an Iranian bank account the brokers gave, and the balance was paid into a bank account the brokers gave while the group was held in Istanbul <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.

<a id="the-path-1"></a>

#### 4.3.2 The path

1. <a id="the-path-1-item-1"></a>**Dubai to Oman by taxi.** By Faridul Alam's account, at the end of 2010 the smugglers put him and several others in a taxi in Dubai that crossed the UAE border into a coastal hill area of Oman though the passengers had no passport or visa <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.
2. <a id="the-path-1-item-2"></a>**Oman to Iran by boat.** From there about thirty young men were put on a boat to Bandar Abbas in Iran, where another Bangladeshi broker received them <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.
3. <a id="the-path-1-item-3"></a>**Iran to Istanbul.** He said the group was kept about seven days in Iran and taken by taxi and then bus into Turkey, that police took them off the bus on the way to Istanbul and sent them back to the border six times, and that the broker's men sent them again each time until they reached Istanbul on the seventh attempt <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.
4. <a id="the-path-1-item-4"></a>**Istanbul to Greece by lorry.** He said that in Istanbul many people were kept crammed in one room and not allowed outside for more than a month, and that about a hundred people were then loaded for Greece into a closed lorry-like vehicle with room for 25 to 30 at most <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.

Sujan Mia of Simtola village, Chhatak upazila, said on 18 April 2026 that he first went from Bangladesh to Oman for construction work, moved to Dubai after a problem with his iqama (residence permit) and then worked in Qatar, and later returned to Dubai to head for Europe, going through Oman to Iran, Turkey, Greece, North Macedonia and Serbia, working illegally to survive <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>. Both men later went on through the Balkans (see [The Balkans and Eastern Europe](#the-balkans-and-eastern-europe)) <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364), [N92](#n92)</sup>.

<a id="what-happens"></a>

#### 4.3.3 What happens

- **Arrival in Greece.** By Faridul Alam's account, Greek soldiers or border guards caught the group where the lorry dropped them and put them in jail, UN refugee agency staff then moved them to a refugee camp, and after about ten days they were given a document called a "stay paper" and took a bus to Athens with the EUR 60 per person the smugglers had handed them in Istanbul <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.
- **A false nationality.** He said he presented himself as Rohingya in all his asylum applications in Greece because the smuggling ring's brokers advised that asylum might be easier that way; the court did not accept his application and stamped his stay paper, which let him stay one month, and he never got asylum in Greece despite four or five interviews <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.
- **Nothing to send home.** He said he lived about four years in Athens hawking and doing manual work, with police at times detaining him and taking his goods, and that what he earned fed him and left nothing to send to his family in Bangladesh <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.
- **Refused again further on.** His asylum applications were rejected in Germany, where he lived on EUR 350 a month from a refugee camp from 2015 to 2018, and again in Paris, where the process took from 2018 to 2021; in December 2024 he was cooking for a mess of Bangladeshis in La Courneuve, Paris <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.
- **No passport to come home on.** He said the Bangladesh embassy in Paris took his application and fingerprints for a passport and, after a long wait, told him it could not issue a new passport unless he submitted his old Bangladeshi one, which he had thrown away when he set out from Dubai because, he said, an asylum application cannot be made while holding it <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>.

<a id="the-balkans-and-eastern-europe"></a>

### 4.4 The Balkans and Eastern Europe

<a id="how-it-is-sold-2"></a>

#### 4.4.1 How it is sold

The product is a "work permit" or "work-permit visa" for an Eastern European country, sold by individuals and some agencies for lakhs of taka <sup>[N357 (2021)](#n357)</sup>. Victims report that the papers the agency hands over are declared fake once the file reaches the embassy, and that one permit is resold to several people with the name changed <sup>[N357 (2021)](#n357)</sup>. In April 2026 thirteen countries' missions, including Italy, Hungary and Germany, jointly warned Bangladeshis against false documents, unlicensed agents and payments to anyone claiming special access; none of them works with any agent <sup>[N393](#n393)</sup>. A Romanian job sold for EUR 11,000 (about Tk 15.4 lakh) turned out to be undeclared building work, 12 hours a day, 7 days a week <sup>[N394](#n394), [167](#o167)</sup>. Thousands of Bangladeshis have paid up to EUR 20,000 for Italian work permits under Italy's quota scheme (Decreto Flussi) that never came <sup>[N395](#n395)</sup>.

<a id="the-path-2"></a>

#### 4.4.2 The path

- **Legal entry, then onward.** Workers go legally to a cheaper EU country such as Romania, lose the job or the papers, and move on to Italy <sup>[N395](#n395)</sup>. One man paid EUR 300 more for the Romanian nulla osta (permission) he needed to change jobs, then flew to Italy to join a cousin, Romania now being in the Schengen area (the EU zone without border checks) <sup>[N394](#n394)</sup>.
- **Overland from Greece.** Faridul Alam, who had reached Greece from Dubai through Iran and Turkey (see [From the Gulf overland through Iran and Turkey to Greece](#from-the-gulf-overland-through-iran-and-turkey-to-greece)), said that at the end of 2014 he left Athens by bus with two or three other Bangladeshis for Macedonia, then Serbia, then Hungary, where the bus of Afghan, Pakistani and Bangladeshi refugees was taken to a refugee camp; he then left for Austria and Germany, and in the Czech Republic police held him eight to ten days and took the money and papers in his bag and pockets and did not return them <sup>[N364 (2024)](#n364)</sup>. Sujan Mia, who had also come from the Gulf, said that, finding no chance of legal work in the countries he passed through, he went back to Greece and from there travelled through Bulgaria, Albania and Bosnia and across Italy to France, where he worked about four years and eight months and tried to regularise; though he paid tax and completed the process, he was caught before he got legal papers and came back to Bangladesh <sup>[N92](#n92)</sup>.
- **Overland through Bosnia.** In September 2026 Bosnian border police stopped 96 people, 36 of them Bangladeshis, crossing illegally at Bistrica near Gradiska, and seized two boats <sup>[N396](#n396)</sup>.
- **Refused, then the boat.** A Cumilla man spent three years trying Serbia, Romania, Slovenia and Belarus legally, was refused every time, then went irregularly <sup>[N361](#n361)</sup>.
- **Through Central Asia.** Brokers have taken men sold "Poland" or "Europe" to Kyrgyzstan instead (see [Kyrgyzstan and Central Asia as a stepping stone](#kyrgyzstan-and-central-asia-as-a-stepping-stone)) <sup>[N225 (2023)](#n225), [N162](#n162)</sup>.
- **Fake visa on the plane.** In December 2022 a Bangladeshi flying from Abu Dhabi to Krakow, Poland, on a fully forged Polish work visa bought for about USD 2,000 was refused entry and charged <sup>[177 (2022)](#o177)</sup>.

<a id="what-happens-1"></a>

#### 4.4.3 What happens

- **Serbia:** more than 40 Bangladeshis recruited by Bangladeshi agencies reached the Linglong tyre factory in June 2025; rights groups reported possible trafficking <sup>[N397](#n397)</sup>. Many had borrowed up to USD 10,000 and had passports taken on arrival; workers said they were paid below Serbia's minimum wage of about USD 660 a month, and their contracts named a Serbian middleman company, not the factory <sup>[N397](#n397)</sup>.
- **Eastern Europe generally:** workers find the pay lower than promised, and lower still after tax <sup>[N357 (2021)](#n357)</sup>. Employers in Poland, Hungary, Bulgaria, Slovenia, Croatia, Kosovo, Albania and Montenegro are increasingly reluctant to hire Bangladeshis because many leave for Western Europe <sup>[N388](#n388)</sup>.
- **Being sent back:** about 5,000 Bangladeshis have been returned from Europe under a 2017 return agreement, most from Cyprus, Italy, Romania and France <sup>[N398](#n398)</sup>.
- **Visa refusals:** in 2025 Schengen consulates in Bangladesh refused 54.5% of applications, and the Italian ambassador links high refusals to fake documents <sup>[178](#o178), [179](#o179)</sup>. A forged document leads to refusal and a flag in the Schengen system that affects all future EU travel <sup>[180](#o180)</sup>.

<a id="the-numbers-2"></a>

#### 4.4.4 The numbers

- **Legal flows:** 18,220 Bangladeshis migrated legally to Europe from January to 25 June 2026 <sup>[N388](#n388)</sup>.
- **The risky "new" countries:** from January 2025 to 30 October 2026, 44,122 Bangladeshis got BMET clearance for 12 countries the Ministry now restricts, 27,647 of them for Cambodia, Russia, Laos and Belarus <sup>[N399](#n399)</sup>. Cambodia, Russia, Laos and Belarus are on the lowest tier (Tier 3) of the US State Department's 2026 Trafficking in Persons report <sup>[N27](#n27)</sup>.
- **Visa processing:** applications for several European countries are handled by embassies outside Bangladesh, mainly in India <sup>[N388](#n388)</sup>.

<a id="the-law-1"></a>

#### 4.4.5 The law

- **Bangladesh:** on 4 November 2026 the Ministry of Expatriates' Welfare told BMET to give no emigration clearance for 12 countries without the Ministry's recruitment permission or approval <sup>[N13](#n13)</sup>. Every published list names Serbia, Moldova, Belarus, Japan, Iraq, Laos, Vietnam, Ukraine and Libya; most also name Russia and Cambodia; the rest differ between reports (Congo, Kosovo, Croatia or Bosnia) <sup>[N13](#n13), [N400](#n400), [N401](#n401), [N27](#n27)</sup>. As Prothom Alo reported it on 5 November 2026, the letter to BMET's Director General names Serbia, Moldova, Belarus, Congo, Russia, Japan, Iraq, Laos, Vietnam, Cambodia, Ukraine and Libya, and BAIRA (the Bangladesh Association of International Recruiting Agencies) sent a copy of the instruction to all its member agencies the same day <sup>[N182](#n182)</sup>. Getting or selling a demand letter, visa or work permit by illegal means is a crime (see [The law in Bangladesh](#the-law-in-bangladesh)) <sup>[26](#o26)</sup>.
- **EU:** forging a visa or using one is punished in Italy by 1 to 6 years in prison <sup>[181](#o181)</sup>.

<a id="where-to-get-help-1"></a>

#### 4.4.6 Where to get help

Report a fake permit offer to BMET or the CID (see [Where victims and families get help](#where-victims-and-families-get-help)) <sup>[10](#o10), [N365](#n365)</sup>. People in the sector told Prothom Alo in November 2026 that workers interested in jobs in the 12 restricted countries should hold back from any payment or final decision until the appointment letter, the Ministry's approval and the emigration clearance have all been confirmed <sup>[N182](#n182)</sup>.

> **Need to know**
> - What is the street price in 2026 of a Serbia, Croatia or Romania "work permit" sold in Dhaka or Sylhet, and how is it paid?
> - Is anyone still being sent through Minsk to cross into Poland or Lithuania, and what does that leg cost?
> - What is the exact list of 12 countries in the Ministry's 4 November 2026 letter?

<a id="the-united-states-through-south-america-and-mexico"></a>

### 4.5 The United States through South America and Mexico

<a id="how-it-is-sold-3"></a>

#### 4.5.1 How it is sold
